\frac{\partial}{\partial g_{\alpha k}}\operatorname{tr}(\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\mathbf{R}^{(ij)}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\mathbf{R}^{(ij)})=-\frac{4\sqrt{D_{k,p}^{0}\lambda_{B,\alpha,p}}}{n}\bm{e}_{\alpha}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{(ij)}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\mathbf{R}^{(ij)}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\mathbf{R}^{(ij)}\bm{x}_{k}.

Using these identities,
D_{i,p}^{0}\leq\mu_{-}^{-1}
,

\|\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\omega_{+}
, and

\|\mathbf{R}^{(ij)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\rho_{0}^{-1}
, repeated
Cauchy–Schwarz followed by the Gaussian second- and fourth-moment
identities gives the conditional gradient bound

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{G}_{B}}\left[\sum_{\alpha=1}^{p-r}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left|\frac{\partial(\widetilde{T}_{ab,n}-M_{ab,n})}{\partial g_{\alpha k}}\right|^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathbf{D}_{p}^{0}\right]\leq\frac{C}{n}.

Here the terms with
k\in\{i,j\}
are controlled by the row energies
of the two resolvents, whereas the terms with
k\notin\{i,j\}
contain
an additional factor
n^{-1}
; after summing over
(i,j,k)
both classes
are bounded by
C/n
. The constant is uniform because
p/n
is bounded
and all Gaussian moments involved are finite. Since the conditional
mean of
\widetilde{T}_{ab,n}-M_{ab,n}
is zero, the Gaussian
Poincaré inequality

(
Boucheron et al. 2013
, Theorem 3.20, p. 72)

therefore yields

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{G}_{B}}\left[(\widetilde{T}_{ab,n}-M_{ab,n})^{2}\mid\mathbf{D}_{p}^{0}\right]\leq\frac{C}{n}.

Consequently,

\widetilde{T}_{ab,n}-M_{ab,n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

It remains to compute
M_{ab,n}
. Let

S_{n,\rho}=\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}(\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n}),\qquad\overline{A}_{a,n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\frac{(D_{i,p}^{0})^{1+a/2}}{(1+\delta_{n,\rho}^{D}D_{i,p}^{0})^{2}}.

Deleting two columns is a rank-two perturbation. The resolvent identity,

\|\mathbf{R}^{(ij)}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\rho_{0}^{-1}
,
and
\|\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\omega_{+}
imply the deterministic bound

\left|\operatorname{tr}(\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\mathbf{R}^{(ij)}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\mathbf{R}^{(ij)})-\operatorname{tr}(\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n})\right|\leq C.

Since all
D_{i,p}^{0}
and
\gamma_{i}
are uniformly bounded, summing this
bound over
i\neq j
gives an
O(n^{-1})
contribution. Restoring the
omitted diagonal
i=j
also costs
O(n^{-1})
. Consequently,

M_{ab,n}=S_{n,\rho}\overline{A}_{a,n}\overline{A}_{b,n}+O(n^{-1}),

and therefore

\displaystyle T_{ab,n}-S_{n,\rho}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\frac{(D_{i,p}^{0})^{1+a/2}}{(1+\delta_{n,\rho}^{D}D_{i,p}^{0})^{2}}\right\}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j}\frac{(D_{j,p}^{0})^{1+b/2}}{(1+\delta_{n,\rho}^{D}D_{j,p}^{0})^{2}}\right\}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

Lemma
B.2
,
(
B.6
), and Bernstein’s inequality
(
Boucheron et al. 2013
, Theorem 2.10 and Corollary 2.11, pp. 36–37)
now give

T_{ab,n}=\frac{u_{p,\rho}^{\star}A_{a,p,\rho}^{\star}A_{b,p,\rho}^{\star}}{\rho^{2}-u_{p,\rho}^{\star}v_{p,\rho}^{\star}}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/4}).

Together with (
B.23
), this proves
(
B.16
).

Finally,

s_{1,p}-b_{1,p,\rho}^{\star}=\int\frac{d^{1/2}}{1+\delta_{p,\rho}d}\,\,\mathrm{d}F_{D,p}(d),\qquad s_{2,p}-b_{2,p,\rho}^{\star}=\int\frac{d}{1+\delta_{p,\rho}d}\,\,\mathrm{d}F_{D,p}(d).

Since
0<D_{p}^{0}\leq d_{+}:=\mu_{-}^{-1}
and

c_{\delta}\leq\delta_{p,\rho}\leq C_{\delta}
,

\displaystyle s_{1,p}-b_{1,p,\rho}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{(D_{p}^{0})^{1/2}}{1+\delta_{p,\rho}D_{p}^{0}}\right\}\geq\frac{\mathbb{E}(D_{p}^{0})^{1/2}}{1+C_{\delta}d_{+}}\geq\frac{1}{1+C_{\delta}d_{+}},

\displaystyle\kappa_{p,\rho}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\delta_{p,\rho}D_{p}^{0}}{1+\delta_{p,\rho}D_{p}^{0}}\right\}\geq\frac{c_{\delta}\mathbb{E}D_{p}^{0}}{1+C_{\delta}d_{+}}\geq\frac{c_{\delta}}{1+C_{\delta}d_{+}}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle D_{p,\rho}^{\star}

\displaystyle\geq m_{-}^{2}(1+C_{\delta}d_{+})^{-2}=:D_{-}>0,

\displaystyle D_{p,\rho}^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq m_{1}^{2}s_{1,p}^{2}+m_{2}s_{2,p}\leq m_{1}^{2}d_{+}+m_{2}d_{+}=:D_{+}<\infty.

Consequently,

\frac{c_{\delta}}{(1+C_{\delta}d_{+})D_{+}}\leq\mu_{p,\rho}^{\star}:=\frac{\kappa_{p,\rho}^{\star}}{D_{p,\rho}^{\star}}\leq D_{-}^{-1}.

The bulk spectrum also gives

u_{p,\rho}^{\star}\geq\frac{c_{B,n}\omega_{-}^{2}}{(1+C_{\delta}\omega_{+})^{2}}\geq c>0.

Let

\mathcal{E}_{0}=\{\max_{1\leq a\leq r}|G_{a}|\leq 1\}
and

\pi_{0}=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0})>0
. On
\mathcal{E}_{0}
,

Q_{p}^{0}=\mu_{0,p}+\sum_{a=1}^{r}\tau_{a,p}G_{a}^{2}\leq\mu_{0,p}+\sum_{a=1}^{r}\tau_{a,p}=1,\qquad 1\leq D_{p}^{0}\leq d_{+}.

Hence

A_{0,p,\rho}^{\star}=\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{D_{p}^{0}}{(1+\delta_{p,\rho}D_{p}^{0})^{2}}\right\}\geq\frac{\pi_{0}}{(1+C_{\delta}d_{+})^{2}}>0.

Together with

s_{0}\leq\rho^{2}-u_{p,\rho}^{\star}v_{p,\rho}^{\star}\leq\rho_{1}^{2},

these inequalities show that

\Psi_{00,p}^{\star}
is bounded above and away from zero.
The rates in (
B.13
)–(
B.16
) transfer the same
bounds to
D_{n}
,
\mu_{n}
, and
\psi_{00,n}
with probability tending to
one. Write
\bm{g}_{n}=(g_{1,n},g_{2,n},g_{3,n})^{\top}
. Then

g_{1,n}=D_{n}^{-2}(e_{n}-b_{1,n})^{2}\geq c>0,\qquad g_{2,n},g_{3,n}\geq 0

with probability tending to one. The exact covariance calculation in
Lemma
E.2
gives

\displaystyle\sigma_{D,n}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}^{2}\{2g_{1,n}+g_{2,n}(w_{i}+w_{j})+2g_{3,n}w_{i}w_{j}\}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{4g_{1,n}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}^{2}=2g_{1,n}^{2}\psi_{00,n}.

Thus
\sigma_{D,n}^{2}
is bounded away from zero, and
(
B.16
) implies the same lower bound for

(\sigma_{E,p}^{\star})^{2}
. All remaining quantities are bounded
above. The displayed rates and the mean-value theorem prove
(
B.17
)–(
B.18
).
∎

Passage to the limits in Section 2.4.

The compactness in Lemma
B.1
,

c_{B,n}\to c
,
H_{B,p}\Rightarrow H_{B}
, and

F_{D,p}\Rightarrow F_{D}
imply that every convergent subsequence of

(\delta_{p,\rho},\widetilde{\delta}_{p,\rho})
solves
(2.8)–(2.9). Uniqueness gives

\delta_{p,\rho}\to\delta_{\rho},\qquad\widetilde{\delta}_{p,\rho}\to\widetilde{\delta}_{\rho}.

All integrands defining
s_{a,p}
,
b_{a,p,\rho}^{\star}
,

u_{p,\rho}^{\star}
,
v_{p,\rho}^{\star}
, and

A_{a,p,\rho}^{\star}
are uniformly bounded because

0<D_{p}^{0}\leq\mu_{-}^{-1}
. Dominated convergence therefore yields

D_{p,\rho}^{\star}\to D_{\rho},\qquad(\sigma_{E,p}^{\star})^{2}\to\sigma_{E}^{2}(\rho).

∎

Appendix C
Spatial-median expansion under pervasive dependence

C.1
Spatial-median expansion

For later use, write

\bm{U}_{F,i}=\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\bm{U}_{i},\qquad\bm{U}_{B,i}=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{U}_{i},\qquad\bm{Y}_{F,i}=\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\bm{Y}_{i},\qquad\bm{Y}_{B,i}=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{Y}_{i}.

Let

\bm{Z}_{n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{Y}_{i},\qquad\bm{W}_{n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\bm{Y}_{i},\qquad e_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i},\qquad t_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}^{2}.

By Proposition
A.1
, the population derivative of the scaled spatial score is
m_{1}\mathbf{J}_{E,p}
. Hence the population Bahadur term is

\bm{\delta}_{n}^{\star}=\frac{1}{m_{1}\sqrt{n}}\mathbf{J}_{E,p}^{-1}\bm{Z}_{n}.

For the sharper expansion required by the quadratic statistic, define

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{n}^{J}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\bm{U}_{i}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top},

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{J}

\displaystyle=\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{V}_{n}^{J}+\mathbf{V}_{n}^{J}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}-\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{V}_{n}^{J}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p},

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{F,n}

\displaystyle=e_{n}\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{J},\qquad\mathbf{G}_{F,n}=\mathbf{J}_{F,n}^{-1}-e_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{I}_{p}.

(C.1)

The inverses in (
C.1
) exist with probability tending to one. The matrix
\mathbf{G}_{F,n}
has fixed rank. It retains the random factor–bulk block of the sample score Jacobian, whereas the remaining bulk block has operator norm
O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1})
.

Theorem C.1
.

Under Assumption 1 and
H_{0}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\theta}_{0,p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e_{n}\sqrt{n}}\bm{Z}_{n}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}+\bm{r}_{\theta,B,n},

(C.2)

\displaystyle\operatorname{rank}(\mathbf{G}_{F,n})

\displaystyle\leq 2r,\qquad\left\lVert\bm{r}_{\theta,B,n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}),

(C.3)

where

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(C.4)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

(C.5)

The population expansion remains

\widehat{\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\theta}_{0,p}=\bm{\delta}_{n}^{\star}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})

(C.6)

in Euclidean norm. More importantly, for every fixed
\rho\in[\rho_{0},\rho_{1}]
,

n\left|(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\theta}_{0,p})^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\theta}_{0,p})-\frac{1}{ne_{n}^{2}}\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(C.7)

Thus the random sample-Jacobian factor block is retained before ridge suppression; replacing it only by a Euclidean
O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})
remainder would not be sufficient for a
\sqrt{n}
-order limit.

For
\bm{x}\neq\mathbf{0}
, write
r=\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert
,
\bm{u}=\bm{x}/r
, and
\mathbf{P}_{\bm{u}}=\mathbf{I}_{p}-\bm{u}\bm{u}^{\top}
. Direct differentiation gives

\displaystyle D\mathbf{U}_{\bm{x}}[\bm{h}]

\displaystyle=r^{-1}\mathbf{P}_{\bm{u}}\bm{h},

\displaystyle D^{2}\mathbf{U}_{\bm{x}}[\bm{h},\bm{h}]

\displaystyle=r^{-2}\{-2\bm{h}(\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{h})-\bm{u}\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert^{2}+3\bm{u}(\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{h})^{2}\}.

For
\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert\leq r/2
,

\left\lVert D^{3}\mathbf{U}_{\bm{x}-t\bm{h}}[\bm{h},\bm{h},\bm{h}]\right\rVert\leq C\frac{\left\lVert\bm{h}\right\rVert^{3}}{r^{3}},\qquad 0\leq t\leq 1.

Lemma C.1
.

For every fixed integer
1\leq k\leq 6
and deterministic vectors
\bm{a}_{F}\in\operatorname{span}(\mathbf{P}_{F,p})
and
\bm{a}_{B}\in\operatorname{span}(\mathbf{P}_{B,p})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\bm{a}_{F}^{\top}\bm{U}_{i}|^{2k}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{F}\right\rVert^{2k},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\bm{a}_{B}^{\top}\bm{U}_{i}|^{2k}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}p^{-k}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{B}\right\rVert^{2k}.

(C.8)

In addition, for every fixed integer
0\leq k\leq 6
,

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}^{k}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{U}_{i}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}),

(C.9)

while

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}^{k}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\bm{U}_{i}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

(C.10)

Proof.

In the eigenbasis of
\bm{\Omega}_{p}
,

\bm{a}_{B}^{\top}\bm{U}_{i}=\frac{\bm{a}_{B}^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}^{1/2}\bm{G}_{B,i}}{\sqrt{pq_{i,p}}}.

Since

q_{i,p}\geq\frac{\omega_{-}}{p}\left\lVert\bm{G}_{B,i}\right\rVert^{2},

rotational invariance of
\bm{G}_{B,i}/\left\lVert\bm{G}_{B,i}\right\rVert
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\bm{a}_{B}^{\top}\bm{U}_{i}|^{2k}

\displaystyle\leq\omega_{-}^{-k}\mathbb{E}\left|\bm{a}_{B}^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}^{1/2}\frac{\bm{G}_{B,i}}{\left\lVert\bm{G}_{B,i}\right\rVert}\right|^{2k}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}p^{-k}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{B}\right\rVert^{2k},

which proves (
C.8
). The factor inequality follows from
\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\bm{U}_{i}\right\rVert\leq 1
.

For (
C.9
), use
\bm{U}_{B,i}=p^{-1/2}\bm{Y}_{B,i}
. The separable bulk proxy bounds in the proof of Proposition
B.1
,
w_{i}\leq\bar{w}
, and the Gaussian operator-norm bound
(
Vershynin 2018
, Theorem 4.4.5, pp. 90–91)
imply

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}w_{i}^{k}\bm{Y}_{B,i}\bm{Y}_{B,i}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

which yields (
C.9
) after division by
p
.

For the cross block, its squared Frobenius norm has expectation

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}w_{i}^{k}\bm{U}_{F,i}\bm{U}_{B,i}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[w_{i}^{2k}\left\lVert\bm{U}_{F,i}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{U}_{B,i}\right\rVert^{2}\right]\leq\frac{\bar{w}^{2k}}{n}.

For every factor coordinate
a\leq r
and bulk coordinate
j>r
,

w_{i}^{k}U_{ai}U_{ji}
is an odd function of
G_{ai}
(and also of

G_{ji}
), whereas its denominator and
w_{i}
are even in every Gaussian
coordinate. Hence

\mathbb{E}\{w_{i}^{k}\bm{U}_{F,i}\bm{U}_{B,i}^{\top}\}=\mathbf{0}.

Independence across observations then makes all cross terms with distinct
observations vanish. Since
p/n
is bounded, Markov’s inequality gives
(
C.10
).
∎

Let

\mathbf{J}_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\bm{U}_{i}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top})=e_{n}\mathbf{I}_{p}-\frac{1}{np}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\bm{Y}_{i}\bm{Y}_{i}^{\top}.

Lemma C.2
.

Under Assumption 1,

\|\mathbf{J}_{n}-m_{1}\mathbf{J}_{E,p}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

(C.11)

\|\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}-(m_{1}\mathbf{J}_{E,p})^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

(C.12)

and

\mathbb{P}\{\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{J}_{n})<m_{-}j_{-}/2\}\to 0.

(C.13)

In addition,

\mathbb{P}(e_{n}<m_{-}/2)\to 0.

(C.14)

Proof.

Since
w_{i}=\xi_{i}\ell_{i,p}
, where only
\xi_{i}
(not
w_{i}
) is
independent of the Gaussian direction,

\mathbb{E}\mathbf{J}_{n}=\mathbb{E}\{w_{i}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\bm{U}_{i}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top})\}=m_{1}\mathbf{J}_{E,p}.

Split the centered matrix into factor–factor, factor–bulk, and bulk–bulk blocks. The factor–factor block has fixed dimension. Every entry is an average of independent, centered, bounded variables, so

\|\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}(\mathbf{J}_{n}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{J}_{n})\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

For the cross block, coordinatewise Gaussian sign symmetry, rather than
global central symmetry, gives

\mathbb{E}\{w_{i}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\bm{U}_{i}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\}=\mathbf{0}
.
Lemma
C.1
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\bm{U}_{i}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\{w_{i}^{2}\|\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\bm{U}_{i}\|^{2}\|\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{U}_{i}\|^{2}\}

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{-1}=O(p^{-1}).

Thus the cross-block operator norm is
O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})
.

For the bulk block, put

\bm{V}_{i}=w_{i}^{1/2}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{U}_{i}
. Since

\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}(\mathbf{J}_{n}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{J}_{n})\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}=(e_{n}-\mathbb{E}e_{n})\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}-\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\bm{V}_{i}\bm{V}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}(\bm{V}_{i}\bm{V}_{i}^{\top})\right\},

both terms must be retained. The bounded-variable variance calculation gives

e_{n}-\mathbb{E}e_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

The vectors
\bm{V}_{i}
are independent,

\|\bm{V}_{i}\|\leq\bar{w}^{1/2}
, and

\|\mathbb{E}(\bm{V}_{i}\bm{V}_{i}^{\top})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq Cp^{-1}

by (
C.8
). Furthermore,

\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\{(\bm{V}_{i}\bm{V}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\bm{V}_{i}\bm{V}_{i}^{\top})^{2}\}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq Cn/p=O(1).

The matrix Bernstein inequality
(
Tropp 2012
, Theorem 1.4, p. 394)
therefore yields

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\bm{V}_{i}\bm{V}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}(\bm{V}_{i}\bm{V}_{i}^{\top})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log p}}{n}+\frac{\log p}{n}\right)=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{\log p}{p}\right).

Thus the scalar fluctuation
e_{n}-\mathbb{E}e_{n}
is the dominant bulk term, and
combining the three blocks proves (
C.11
).

Proposition
A.1
gives

\lambda_{\min}(m_{1}\mathbf{J}_{E,p})\geq m_{-}j_{-}
. Weyl’s inequality proves
(
C.13
). On that event,

\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}-(m_{1}\mathbf{J}_{E,p})^{-1}=\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}(m_{1}\mathbf{J}_{E,p}-\mathbf{J}_{n})(m_{1}\mathbf{J}_{E,p})^{-1},

which proves (
C.12
).

Finally,

\mathbb{E}e_{n}=m_{1}\mathbb{E}\ell_{i,p}.

Because
x\mapsto x^{-1/2}
is convex and

\mathbb{E}(\bm{V}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{p}\bm{V}_{i})=p^{-1}\operatorname{tr}\bm{\Omega}_{p}=1
,

\mathbb{E}\ell_{i,p}=\mathbb{E}(\bm{V}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{p}\bm{V}_{i})^{-1/2}\geq 1.

The bounded-variable variance bound gives

e_{n}-\mathbb{E}e_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
, proving (
C.14
).
∎

Put

\mathbf{V}_{B,n}^{J}=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{V}_{n}^{J}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}.

Then

\mathbf{J}_{n}=\mathbf{J}_{F,n}-\mathbf{V}_{B,n}^{J},\qquad\mathbf{J}_{F,n}=\mathbf{J}_{n}+\mathbf{V}_{B,n}^{J}.

(C.15)

Lemma C.3
.

Under Assumption 1,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{V}_{n}^{J}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(C.16)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{V}_{n}^{J}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{V}_{B,n}^{J}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

(C.17)

Moreover, with probability tending to one,

\displaystyle\operatorname{rank}(\mathbf{G}_{F,n})

\displaystyle\leq 2r,

(C.18)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(C.19)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}),

(C.20)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}-e_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

(C.21)

Proof.

The block estimates in Lemma
C.1
, with
k=1
, give

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{V}_{n}^{J}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\bm{U}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\bar{w},

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{V}_{n}^{J}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{V}_{B,n}^{J}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

(C.22)

Thus (
C.16
)–(
C.17
) hold.

By Lemma
C.2
, on the event in (
C.13
), (
C.15
) and

\mathbf{V}_{B,n}^{J}\succeq\mathbf{0}
yield

\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{J}_{F,n})\geq\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{J}_{n})\geq m_{-}j_{-}/2,\qquad\left\lVert\mathbf{J}_{F,n}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 4/(m_{-}j_{-}).

(C.23)

Lemma
C.2
, specifically (
C.14
), also gives
e_{n}\geq m_{-}/2
with probability tending to one. With respect to

\mathbb{R}^{p}=\operatorname{span}(\mathbf{P}_{F,p})\mathrm{op}\operatorname{span}(\mathbf{P}_{B,p}),

write

\mathbf{J}_{F,n}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{A}_{J,n}&-\mathbf{C}_{J,n}\\
-\mathbf{C}_{J,n}^{\top}&e_{n}\mathbf{I}_{p-r}\end{pmatrix},

where

\mathbf{A}_{J,n}=e_{n}\mathbf{I}_{r}-\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{n}^{J}\mathbf{P}_{F,p},\qquad\mathbf{C}_{J,n}=\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{n}^{J}\mathbf{P}_{B,p}.

Let

\mathbf{S}_{J,n}=\mathbf{A}_{J,n}-e_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{J,n}\mathbf{C}_{J,n}^{\top}.

The block inverse formula and (
C.23
) give

\mathbf{J}_{F,n}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{S}_{J,n}^{-1}&e_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{J,n}^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{J,n}\\
e_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{J,n}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{J,n}^{-1}&e_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{I}_{p-r}+e_{n}^{-2}\mathbf{C}_{J,n}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{J,n}^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{J,n}\end{pmatrix},

(C.24)

with

\left\lVert\mathbf{S}_{J,n}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\left\lVert\mathbf{C}_{J,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

Subtracting
e_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{I}_{p}
from (
C.24
) proves
(
C.19
)–(
C.20
). Furthermore,

\mathbf{G}_{F,n}=e_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{J}_{F,n}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{J},\qquad\operatorname{rank}(\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{J})\leq 2r,

which proves (
C.18
).

Finally, the inverse identity applied to (
C.15
) yields

\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}-\mathbf{J}_{F,n}^{-1}=\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{B,n}^{J}\mathbf{J}_{F,n}^{-1}.

Equations (
C.13
), (
C.23
), and
(
C.22
) imply

\left\lVert\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}-\mathbf{J}_{F,n}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{4}{m_{-}^{2}j_{-}^{2}}\left\lVert\mathbf{V}_{B,n}^{J}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}),

which proves (
C.21
).
∎

Let
\bm{\delta}_{n}=\widehat{\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\theta}_{0,p}
and
\mathbf{P}_{i}=\mathbf{I}_{p}-\bm{U}_{i}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}
. Multiplying the Taylor expansion by
\sqrt{p}
gives

\widehat{\bm{Y}}_{i}=\bm{Y}_{i}-w_{i}\mathbf{P}_{i}\bm{\delta}_{n}+\frac{w_{i}^{2}}{2\sqrt{p}}\bm{H}_{i}(\bm{\delta}_{n})+\bm{R}_{i}(\bm{\delta}_{n}),

(C.25)

where

\bm{H}_{i}(\bm{d})=-2\bm{d}(\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{d})-\bm{U}_{i}\left\lVert\bm{d}\right\rVert^{2}+3\bm{U}_{i}(\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{d})^{2}

and, whenever
\left\lVert\bm{d}\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{p}/(2w_{i})
,

\left\lVert\bm{R}_{i}(\bm{d})\right\rVert\leq C\frac{w_{i}^{3}}{p}\left\lVert\bm{d}\right\rVert^{3}.

(C.26)

Lemma C.4
.

Let
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
be independent Rademacher variables and let

\mathbb{H}=\mathbb{H}_{n,p}
be a finite-dimensional real Hilbert space, whose dimension may vary with
(n,p)
. Conditionally on
any sigma-field independent of the signs, consider

\mathcal{P}=\sum_{|\mathcal{I}|\leq d}s_{\mathcal{I}}\bm{c}_{\mathcal{I}},\qquad s_{\mathcal{I}}=\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}s_{i},\qquad\bm{c}_{\mathcal{I}}\in\mathbb{H},

where
d
is fixed. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s}\|\mathcal{P}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\mathcal{I}|\leq d}\|\bm{c}_{\mathcal{I}}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2},

(C.27)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s}\|\mathcal{P}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\left\{\sum_{|\mathcal{I}|\leq d}\|\bm{c}_{\mathcal{I}}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\right\}^{2}.

(C.28)

If
\mathcal{Q}=\sum_{|\mathcal{J}|\leq e}s_{\mathcal{J}}\bm{d}_{\mathcal{J}}
is Euclidean-valued and

\mathcal{P}\mathcal{Q}^{\top}=\sum_{\mathcal{K}}s_{\mathcal{K}}\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{K}}
, then

\sum_{\mathcal{K}}\|\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq C_{d,e}\left(\sum_{\mathcal{I}}\|\bm{c}_{\mathcal{I}}\|^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{\mathcal{J}}\|\bm{d}_{\mathcal{J}}\|^{2}\right).

(C.29)

The same conclusions hold for matrix-valued polynomials with the
Frobenius norm and after applying deterministic linear maps of bounded
operator norm.

Proof.

Walsh orthogonality gives (
C.27
). Apply the scalar
degree-
d
Bonami hypercontractive inequality

(
O’Donnell 2014
, Theorem 9.21, p. 262)
to each coordinate. Expanding

\mathbb{E}_{s}\|\mathcal{P}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{4}
as a double sum and applying
Cauchy–Schwarz to each mixed coordinate moment gives the
dimension-free Hilbert-space bound (
C.28
); its constant
depends only on
d
. Parseval,

\|\bm{x}\bm{y}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\|\bm{x}\|\|\bm{y}\|
, Cauchy–Schwarz, and
(
C.28
) yield

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal{K}}\|\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{s}\|\mathcal{P}\mathcal{Q}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\{\mathbb{E}_{s}\|\mathcal{P}\|^{4}\}^{1/2}\{\mathbb{E}_{s}\|\mathcal{Q}\|^{4}\}^{1/2},

which is (
C.29
). The final assertion
follows by applying the corresponding operator-norm bound to every
coefficient.
∎

Lemma C.5
.

Under Assumption 1 and
H_{0}
,

\left\lVert\bm{\delta}_{n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(C.30)

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{w_{i}^{2}}{2\sqrt{p}}\bm{H}_{i}(\bm{\delta}_{n})\right\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}),

(C.31)

and

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{R}_{i}(\bm{\delta}_{n})\right\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

(C.32)

Proof.

Let
\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}=n^{-1}\sum_{i}\bm{Y}_{i}
. Since

\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{R}_{p})=p
,

\mathbb{E}\|\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}\|^{2}=\frac{p}{n}=O(1).

(C.33)

For
\|\bm{d}\|\leq\sqrt{p}/(2\bar{w})
, Taylor’s formula gives

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\bm{Y}}_{i}(\bm{d})=\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}-\mathbf{J}_{n}\bm{d}+\bm{R}_{S,n}(\bm{d}),\qquad\|\bm{R}_{S,n}(\bm{d})\|\leq C\left\{\frac{\|\bm{d}\|^{2}}{\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\|\bm{d}\|^{3}}{p}\right\}.

On the event in (
C.13
), for
\|\bm{d}\|=M
,

\displaystyle\bm{d}^{\top}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\widehat{\bm{Y}}_{i}(\bm{d})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq M\|\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}\|-\frac{m_{-}j_{-}}{4}M^{2}+C\frac{M^{3}}{\sqrt{p}}+C\frac{M^{4}}{p}.

Equation (
C.33
) permits a fixed
M
for which the
right-hand side is negative with probability arbitrarily close to one.
Convexity of
\sum_{i}\|\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{d}\|
then proves
(
C.30
). This tightness also justifies the
ordinary score equation used below. Indeed,

\min_{i\leq n}\|\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{\theta}_{0,p}\|=\min_{i\leq n}\frac{\sqrt{p}}{w_{i}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{p}}{\bar{w}}.

For every fixed
M
, on the event
\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|\leq M
and for all
sufficiently large
p
, the sample median differs from every observation.
The objective is then differentiable at
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}
, and its first-order
condition is exactly
n^{-1}\sum_{i}\widehat{\bm{Y}}_{i}=\mathbf{0}
.

Define

\bm{d}_{L,n}=\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n},\qquad\bm{S}_{2,n}(\bm{d})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{w_{i}^{2}}{2\sqrt{p}}\bm{H}_{i}(\bm{d}),\qquad\bm{S}_{3,n}(\bm{d})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{R}_{i}(\bm{d}).

The score equation is

\mathbf{J}_{n}\bm{\delta}_{n}=\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}+\bm{S}_{2,n}(\bm{\delta}_{n})+\bm{S}_{3,n}(\bm{\delta}_{n}).

On every event on which
\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|\leq M
,

\|\bm{S}_{2,n}(\bm{\delta}_{n})\|\leq C_{M}p^{-1/2},\qquad\|\bm{S}_{3,n}(\bm{\delta}_{n})\|\leq C_{M}p^{-1}.

Together with (
C.13
), this gives the preliminary linearization

\|\bm{\delta}_{n}-\bm{d}_{L,n}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),\qquad\|\bm{d}_{L,n}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

The bilinear map
\mathcal{H}_{i}
defined by

\mathcal{H}_{i}(\bm{a},\bm{b})=-\bm{a}(\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{b})-\bm{b}(\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{a})-\bm{U}_{i}(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b})+3\bm{U}_{i}(\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{a})(\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{b})

satisfies

\bm{H}_{i}(\bm{d})=\mathcal{H}_{i}(\bm{d},\bm{d}),\qquad\|\mathcal{H}_{i}(\bm{a},\bm{b})\|\leq 6\|\bm{a}\|\|\bm{b}\|.

(C.34)

Hence, on the same event,

\displaystyle\|\bm{S}_{2,n}(\bm{\delta}_{n})-\bm{S}_{2,n}(\bm{d}_{L,n})\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{p}}(\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|+\|\bm{d}_{L,n}\|)\|\bm{\delta}_{n}-\bm{d}_{L,n}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

(C.35)

It remains to evaluate
\bm{S}_{2,n}(\bm{d}_{L,n})
. In the remainder of the appendices,
\mathbb{E}_{s}
,
\operatorname{Var}_{s}
, and
\mathbb{P}_{s}
denote conditional expectation, variance, and probability with respect to the Rademacher signs given
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
. Conditional on

\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
, write

\bm{Y}_{i}=s_{i}\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i},\qquad\bm{U}_{i}=s_{i}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{i},\qquad\bm{a}_{i,n}=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i}.

The matrix
\mathbf{J}_{n}
is
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
-measurable because it depends on

\bm{U}_{i}
only through
\bm{U}_{i}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}
. Therefore

\bm{d}_{L,n}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}s_{j}\bm{a}_{j,n}.

(C.36)

Using the Rademacher representation (
C.36
), on the event in (
C.13
),

\displaystyle\max_{j\leq n}\|\bm{a}_{j,n}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\sqrt{p}}{n}=O(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|\bm{a}_{j,n}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1})\leq\frac{C}{n}\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{B}_{n})=C\frac{p}{n}=O(1).

(C.37)

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
denote the map in
(
C.34
) with
\bm{U}_{i}
replaced by

\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{i}
. Since
\bm{H}_{i}(\bm{d})=s_{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{i}(\bm{d},\bm{d})
,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}w_{i}^{2}\bm{H}_{i}(\bm{d}_{L,n})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{n}w_{i}^{2}s_{i}s_{j}s_{k}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{i}(\bm{a}_{j,n},\bm{a}_{k,n}).

(C.38)

Collecting equal Rademacher monomials in (
C.38
)
produces only Walsh chaoses of degrees one and three. If their vector
coefficients are denoted by
\bm{c}_{\ell}
and

\bm{c}_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}
, respectively, then

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\|\bm{c}_{\ell}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n^{2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left\|\sum_{j}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}(\bm{a}_{j,n},\bm{a}_{j,n})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C}{n^{2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left\|\sum_{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{i}(\bm{a}_{i,n},\bm{a}_{\ell,n})\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{C}{n},

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell_{1}<\ell_{2}<\ell_{3}}\|\bm{c}_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j,k}^{\mathrm{distinct}}\{\|\bm{a}_{j,n}\|^{2}\|\bm{a}_{k,n}\|^{2}+\|\bm{a}_{i,n}\|^{2}\|\bm{a}_{k,n}\|^{2}+\|\bm{a}_{i,n}\|^{2}\|\bm{a}_{j,n}\|^{2}\}\leq\frac{C}{n}.

The first inequality uses (
C.34
), Cauchy–Schwarz,
and (
C.37
); the second additionally uses that the
index not appearing in the displayed product has at most
n
choices.
Orthogonality of distinct Walsh monomials now gives

\mathbb{E}_{s}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}w_{i}^{2}\bm{H}_{i}(\bm{d}_{L,n})\right\|^{2}=\sum_{\ell}\|\bm{c}_{\ell}\|^{2}+\sum_{\ell_{1}<\ell_{2}<\ell_{3}}\|\bm{c}_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{3}}\|^{2}\leq\frac{C}{n}.

Thus

\|\bm{S}_{2,n}(\bm{d}_{L,n})\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}\{(np)^{-1/2}\}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

Combining this relation with (
C.35
) proves
(
C.31
).

Finally, (
C.26
),
w_{i}\leq\bar{w}
, and
(
C.30
) give

\left\|\bm{S}_{3,n}(\bm{\delta}_{n})\right\|\leq\frac{C\bar{w}^{3}}{p}\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|^{3}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}),

which proves (
C.32
).
∎

Lemma C.6
.

Under Assumption 1,

\displaystyle\lambda_{r}(\mathbf{B}_{n})

\displaystyle\geq c_{0}p\quad(r\geq 1),

\displaystyle\lambda_{r+1}(\mathbf{B}_{n})

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(C.39)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

(C.40)

When
r\geq 1
, the first inequality holds with probability tending to
one; when
r=0
, the two factor-resolvent assertions are vacuous.

Proof.

Put
\mathbf{Y}_{n}=\mathbf{Y}/\sqrt{n}
. If
r=0
, then

\mathbf{Y}_{n}=\mathbf{P}_{B,p}\mathbf{Y}_{B,n}
and the separable bulk comparison in
Proposition
B.1
gives

\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{B}_{n})=\|\mathbf{Y}_{B,n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
; all remaining
factor assertions are vacuous. Hence assume
r\geq 1
and decompose the
rows as

\mathbf{Y}_{n}=\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\mathbf{Y}_{F,n}+\mathbf{P}_{B,p}\mathbf{Y}_{B,n},\qquad\mathbf{Y}_{F,n}=\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{n},\qquad\mathbf{Y}_{B,n}=\mathbf{P}_{B,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{n}.

The
r\times r
matrix
p^{-1}\mathbf{Y}_{F,n}\mathbf{Y}_{F,n}^{\top}
has entries

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\sqrt{\tau_{a,p}\tau_{b,p}}G_{ai}G_{bi}}{q_{i,p}},\qquad 1\leq a,b\leq r.

The off-diagonal expectations vanish by sign symmetry. Proposition
A.1
and the fixed-dimensional variance bound give

\left\|p^{-1}\mathbf{Y}_{F,n}\mathbf{Y}_{F,n}^{\top}-\operatorname{diag}(a_{1,p},\ldots,a_{r,p})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

Since
a_{a,p}\geq\tau_{-}\mathbb{E}\{G_{a}^{2}/(1+\sum_{b}\tau_{+}G_{b}^{2})\}>0
uniformly in
p
,

s_{r}(\mathbf{Y}_{F,n})\geq c\sqrt{p},\qquad s_{1}(\mathbf{Y}_{F,n})\leq C\sqrt{p}

with probability tending to one. The separable bulk comparison in
Proposition
B.1
gives

\left\lVert\mathbf{Y}_{B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(C.41)

Weyl’s singular-value inequality applied to

\mathbf{Y}_{n}=\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\mathbf{Y}_{F,n}+\mathbf{P}_{B,p}\mathbf{Y}_{B,n}
yields

s_{r}(\mathbf{Y}_{n})\geq c\sqrt{p}-O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad s_{r+1}(\mathbf{Y}_{n})\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{Y}_{B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Because
\mathbf{B}_{n}=\mathbf{Y}_{n}\mathbf{Y}_{n}^{\top}
, these relations prove
(
C.39
).

Let
\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}_{F,n}
denote the spectral projector of
\mathbf{B}_{n}

associated with its largest
r
eigenvalues. The unperturbed matrix

\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\mathbf{Y}_{F,n}
has left singular space

\operatorname{span}(\mathbf{P}_{F,p})
, its
r
th singular value is at least

c\sqrt{p}
, and the perturbation in (
C.41
) has
operator norm
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Wedin’s sin–theta theorem
(
Stewart and Sun 1990
, Theorem V.4.1, pp. 260–262)
therefore gives

\left\lVert(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}_{F,n})\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

On
\operatorname{span}(\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}_{F,n})
, the resolvent eigenvalues
are at most
(c_{0}p+\rho_{0})^{-1}
; on its orthogonal complement they are
at most
\rho_{0}^{-1}
. Hence

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c_{0}p+\rho_{0}}+\rho_{0}^{-1}\left\lVert(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}_{F,n})\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c_{0}p+\rho_{0}}+\rho_{0}^{-1}\left\lVert(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}_{F,n})\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

This proves (
C.40
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
C.1
.

The score equation and (
C.25
) give

\mathbf{J}_{n}\bm{\delta}_{n}=\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}+\bm{r}_{S,n},

(C.42)

where Lemma
C.5
yields

\left\lVert\bm{r}_{S,n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

(C.43)

Let

\mathbf{R}_{J,n}=\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}-e_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{G}_{F,n}.

Lemma
C.3
gives

\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{J,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

(C.44)

Since
\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}=\bm{Z}_{n}/\sqrt{n}
and

\left\lVert\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, equations
(
C.42
)–(
C.44
) imply

\displaystyle\bm{\delta}_{n}

\displaystyle=(e_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{I}_{p}+\mathbf{G}_{F,n}+\mathbf{R}_{J,n})\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}+\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}\bm{r}_{S,n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e_{n}\sqrt{n}}\bm{Z}_{n}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}+\bm{r}_{\theta,B,n},

(C.45)

where

\left\lVert\bm{r}_{\theta,B,n}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{J,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert\bm{r}_{S,n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

This proves (
C.2
)–(
C.3
).
Equations (
C.18
)–(
C.20
) prove
(
C.4
)–(
C.5
).

For the population expansion, (
C.12
),
(
C.42
), and (
C.43
) give

\displaystyle\bm{\delta}_{n}-\frac{1}{m_{1}}\mathbf{J}_{E,p}^{-1}\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}={}

\displaystyle\{\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}-(m_{1}\mathbf{J}_{E,p})^{-1}\}\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}+\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}\bm{r}_{S,n},

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\delta}_{n}-\frac{1}{m_{1}}\mathbf{J}_{E,p}^{-1}\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}\right\|={}

\displaystyle O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

which proves (
C.6
).

It remains to prove the quadratic replacement. Put

\mathbf{D}_{s,p}=\sqrt{p}\,\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}+\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}.

Lemmas
C.6
and
C.3
give the
block orders

\mathbf{Q}_{n}=\begin{pmatrix}O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1})&O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})\\
O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})&O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\end{pmatrix},\qquad\mathbf{G}_{F,n}=\begin{pmatrix}O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)&O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})\\
O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})&O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1})\end{pmatrix}.

(C.46)

Here every entry denotes the operator norm of the corresponding block.
Therefore

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{D}_{s,p}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{s,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(C.47)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{D}_{s,p}^{-1}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\mathbf{D}_{s,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(C.48)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{D}_{s,p}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\mathbf{D}_{s,p}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

It follows that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{D}_{s,p}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\mathbf{D}_{s,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(C.49)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{D}_{s,p}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\mathbf{D}_{s,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(C.50)

and both matrices in (
C.49
)–(
C.50
)
have rank at most
2r
.

By central symmetry, on an enlarged probability space one may write

\bm{Y}_{i}=s_{i}\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i},\qquad\mathbb{P}(s_{i}=1)=\mathbb{P}(s_{i}=-1)=1/2,

where
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
are independent of

\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}=\sigma\{w_{i},\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i}:1\leq i\leq n\}
.
The matrices
\mathbf{Q}_{n}
and
\mathbf{G}_{F,n}
are
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
-measurable. Let

\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}=\mathbf{D}_{s,p}^{-1}(\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{n}).

The factor–factor, factor–bulk, and bulk–bulk bounds used in the proof of
Lemma
C.6
give

\left\|\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\left\lVert\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}/\sqrt{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(C.51)

Define

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{1,n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{s,p}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\mathbf{D}_{s,p}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n},

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{2,n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{s,p}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\mathbf{D}_{s,p}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}.

Equations (
C.49
)–(
C.51
) imply

\operatorname{rank}(\mathbf{C}_{j,n})\leq 2r,\qquad\left\lVert\mathbf{C}_{j,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\lVert\mathbf{C}_{j,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}+|\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{C}_{j,n})|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad j=1,2.

(C.52)

Writing
\bm{s}=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{n})^{\top}
, one has

\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}=\bm{s}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{1,n}\bm{s},\qquad\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}=\bm{s}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{2,n}\bm{s}.

For any
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
-measurable matrix
\mathbf{C}
, the Rademacher identities

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s}(\bm{s}^{\top}\mathbf{C}\bm{s})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{C}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}_{s}(\bm{s}^{\top}\mathbf{C}\bm{s})

\displaystyle=2\sum_{i\neq j}\left\{\frac{C_{ij}+C_{ji}}{2}\right\}^{2}\leq 2\left\lVert(\mathbf{C}+\mathbf{C}^{\top})/2\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

give, by (
C.52
),

\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(C.53)

Let

\bm{\delta}_{M,n}=e_{n}^{-1}\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}+\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}.

Since
e_{n}^{-1}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,

\left\lVert\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, and

\left\lVert\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,

\left\lVert\bm{\delta}_{M,n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Equations (
C.45
) and
(
C.53
) yield

\displaystyle n\left\{\bm{\delta}_{M,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{\delta}_{M,n}-e_{n}^{-2}\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=2e_{n}^{-1}\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}+\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(C.54)

The remaining terms satisfy

\displaystyle 2n|\bm{\delta}_{M,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{r}_{\theta,B,n}|

\displaystyle\leq 2n\rho_{0}^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{\delta}_{M,n}\right\rVert\left\lVert\bm{r}_{\theta,B,n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n/p)=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle n\bm{r}_{\theta,B,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{r}_{\theta,B,n}

\displaystyle\leq n\rho_{0}^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{r}_{\theta,B,n}\right\rVert^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n/p^{2})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

(C.55)

Combining (
C.54
)–(
C.55
)
proves (
C.7
).
∎

Appendix D
Finite-rank SSCM expansion and Woodbury algebra

D.1
Finite-rank correction of the spatial-sign covariance matrix

Introduce

\mathbf{H}_{n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(\bm{Z}_{n},\bm{W}_{n}),\qquad\mathbf{C}_{n}=\begin{pmatrix}t_{n}/e_{n}^{2}&-1/e_{n}\\
-1/e_{n}&0\end{pmatrix}.

The matrix
\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}
contains the two universal cross-products and the leading quadratic product generated by estimating the center. Terms that touch the pervasive eigenspace are collected in a separate fixed-rank matrix.

Theorem D.1
.

Under Assumption 1 and
H_{0}
, there exist symmetric random matrices
\mathbf{F}_{n}
and
\mathbf{E}_{n}
such that

\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}=\mathbf{B}_{n}+\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}+\mathbf{F}_{n}+\mathbf{E}_{n},

(D.1)

where

\operatorname{rank}(\mathbf{F}_{n})\leq 2r,\qquad\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{F}_{n}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}=\mathbf{0}.

(D.2)

Let

\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}=(\mathbf{B}_{n}+\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}.

Then, for every fixed
\rho\in[\rho_{0},\rho_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

(D.3)

Moreover,

\displaystyle T_{n}(\rho)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{e_{n}^{2}}\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}

(D.4)

\displaystyle\times(\mathbf{B}_{n}+\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{Z}_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\log p).

The full perturbation
\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}+\mathbf{F}_{n}
has rank at most
2+2r
and admits a
(2+2r)
-dimensional Woodbury representation. The factor correction contributes
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and the pure-bulk Taylor remainder contributes
O_{\mathbb{P}}(\log p)
; both are
o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{1/2})
.

D.2
Two-dimensional leading Woodbury reduction

Set

x_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{Z}_{n},\qquad y_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{W}_{n},\qquad z_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\bm{W}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{W}_{n}.

For
x,y,z\in\mathbb{R}
,
e>0
, and
t>0
, define

\displaystyle L(x,y,z,e,t)

\displaystyle=e^{2}+tx-2ey+y^{2}-xz,

\displaystyle f(x,y,z,e,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{x}{L(x,y,z,e,t)}.

Lemma D.1
.

Under Assumption 1 and
H_{0}
,

L(x_{n},y_{n},z_{n},e_{n},t_{n})=D_{n}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

Consequently, with probability tending to one,

\mathbf{C}_{n}^{-1}+\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}

is invertible with bounded inverse, and

\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}=(\mathbf{B}_{n}+\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}

exists and satisfies
\|\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Proof.

On the event
e_{n}\geq m_{-}/2
,

\mathbf{C}_{n}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-e_{n}\\
-e_{n}&-t_{n}\end{pmatrix}.

Conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
, write

\bm{Y}_{i}=s_{i}\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i}
and let

a_{ij}
be the entries of the unsigned companion matrix. Direct
expansion gives

\displaystyle x_{n}-\kappa_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}s_{i}s_{j},

\displaystyle y_{n}-b_{1,n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}(w_{i}+w_{j})s_{i}s_{j},

\displaystyle z_{n}-b_{2,n}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}w_{i}w_{j}s_{i}s_{j}.

Since
0\preceq\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n}\preceq\mathbf{I}_{n}
and
w_{i}\leq\bar{w}
,

\operatorname{Var}_{s}(x_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})+\operatorname{Var}_{s}(y_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})+\operatorname{Var}_{s}(z_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})\leq\frac{C}{n}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j}a_{ij}^{2}\right\}\leq\frac{C}{n}.

Hence

x_{n}=\kappa_{n}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}),\quad y_{n}=b_{1,n}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}),\quad z_{n}=b_{2,n}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

The five arguments are bounded in probability, and therefore

\displaystyle L(x_{n},y_{n},z_{n},e_{n},t_{n})

\displaystyle=e_{n}^{2}+t_{n}\kappa_{n}-2e_{n}b_{1,n}+b_{1,n}^{2}-\kappa_{n}b_{2,n}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=D_{n}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

Proposition
B.1
gives
D_{n}\geq C_{1}
with probability
tending to one. If

\mathbf{G}_{n}=\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}=\begin{pmatrix}x_{n}&y_{n}\\
y_{n}&z_{n}\end{pmatrix},

then

\det(\mathbf{C}_{n}^{-1}+\mathbf{G}_{n})=x_{n}(z_{n}-t_{n})-(y_{n}-e_{n})^{2}=-L(x_{n},y_{n},z_{n},e_{n},t_{n}).

All entries of
\mathbf{C}_{n}^{-1}+\mathbf{G}_{n}
are
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, so its inverse has
operator norm
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. The Woodbury identity now gives

\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}=\mathbf{Q}_{n}-\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}(\mathbf{C}_{n}^{-1}+\mathbf{G}_{n})^{-1}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}.

Because
\|\mathbf{Q}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\rho_{0}^{-1}
and

\|\mathbf{H}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, the displayed identity proves the final
bound without invoking Proposition
D.2
.
∎

Proposition D.2
.

Under Assumption 1 and
H_{0}
,

T_{n}(\rho)=nf(x_{n},y_{n},z_{n},e_{n},t_{n})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(\log p).

(D.5)

For every fixed
\rho\in[\rho_{0},\rho_{1}]
, the denominator
L(x_{n},y_{n},z_{n},e_{n},t_{n})
is bounded away from zero with probability tending to one.

The representation has the same five scalar arguments as in the bounded-spectrum case. Pervasive dependence enters their joint distribution through the heterogeneous diagonal structure of the companion resolvent.

Let

\bm{\delta}_{0,n}=e_{n}^{-1}\bm{Z}_{n}/\sqrt{n}.

Expanding (
C.25
) and collecting the two terms linear in
\bm{\delta}_{0,n}
and the leading quadratic term gives

-\frac{\bm{W}_{n}\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}+\bm{Z}_{n}\bm{W}_{n}^{\top}}{ne_{n}}+\frac{t_{n}\bm{Z}_{n}\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}}{ne_{n}^{2}}=\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}.

Define the exact residual

\mathbf{L}_{n}=\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}-\mathbf{B}_{n}-\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}.

(D.6)

Its part touching the factor subspace and its pure bulk part are

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{n}

\displaystyle=\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{L}_{n}+\mathbf{L}_{n}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}-\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{L}_{n}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p},

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{n}

\displaystyle=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{L}_{n}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}.

(D.7)

Then (
D.1
) is exact. Put

\bm{d}_{0,n}=e_{n}^{-1}\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n},\qquad\bm{r}_{i,n}=\widehat{\bm{Y}}_{i}-\bm{Y}_{i}+w_{i}\bm{d}_{0,n}.

The definition of
\mathbf{L}_{n}
is equivalently

\mathbf{L}_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\{(\bm{Y}_{i}-w_{i}\bm{d}_{0,n})\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\top}+\bm{r}_{i,n}(\bm{Y}_{i}-w_{i}\bm{d}_{0,n})^{\top}+\bm{r}_{i,n}\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\top}\}.

(D.8)

Lemma D.2
.

Let

\mathbf{A}_{F,n}=\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{L}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p},\qquad\mathbf{V}_{F,n}=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{L}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}.

Then

\mathbf{F}_{n}=(\mathbf{P}_{F,p},\mathbf{V}_{F,n})\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{A}_{F,n}&\mathbf{I}_{r}\\
\mathbf{I}_{r}&\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{P}_{F,p},\mathbf{V}_{F,n})^{\top},

so that
\operatorname{rank}(\mathbf{F}_{n})\leq 2r
. Moreover,

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{F,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\left\lVert\mathbf{V}_{F,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.9)

The second order in (
D.9
) cannot in general be sharpened to

O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})
when the factor eigenvalues are of order
p
.

Proof.

The displayed factorization follows from

\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{L}_{n}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}=\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\mathbf{A}_{F,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top},\qquad\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{L}_{n}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}=\mathbf{V}_{F,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}.

Put

\bm{d}_{L,n}=\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n},\qquad\bm{d}_{0,n}=e_{n}^{-1}\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n},

and define the polynomial Taylor residual

\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ}=-w_{i}(\bm{d}_{L,n}-\bm{d}_{0,n})+w_{i}\bm{U}_{i}(\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{d}_{L,n})+\frac{w_{i}^{2}}{2\sqrt{p}}\bm{H}_{i}(\bm{d}_{L,n}).

(D.10)

Let
\mathbf{L}_{n}^{\circ}
be obtained from (
D.8
) by
replacing every
\bm{r}_{i,n}
by
\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ}
, and set

\mathbf{A}_{F,n}^{\circ}=\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{L}_{n}^{\circ}\mathbf{P}_{F,p},\qquad\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\circ}=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{L}_{n}^{\circ}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}.

The score equation and
(
C.31
)–(
C.32
) give

\left\lVert\bm{\delta}_{n}-\bm{d}_{L,n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

By (
C.34
), (
C.26
), and

\left\lVert\bm{\delta}_{n}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\bm{d}_{L,n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,

\displaystyle\bm{r}_{i,n}-\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ}={}

\displaystyle-w_{i}(\bm{\delta}_{n}-\bm{d}_{L,n})+w_{i}\bm{U}_{i}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}(\bm{\delta}_{n}-\bm{d}_{L,n})

\displaystyle+\frac{w_{i}^{2}}{2\sqrt{p}}\{\bm{H}_{i}(\bm{\delta}_{n})-\bm{H}_{i}(\bm{d}_{L,n})\}+\bm{R}_{i}(\bm{\delta}_{n}),

\displaystyle\max_{i\leq n}\left\lVert\bm{r}_{i,n}-\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}),\qquad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\lVert\bm{r}_{i,n}-\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-2}).

(D.11)

Write
\mathbf{X}_{0,n}=(\bm{Y}_{i}-w_{i}\bm{d}_{0,n})_{i=1}^{n}
and

\mathbf{R}_{\eta,n}=(\bm{r}_{i,n}-\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ})_{i=1}^{n}
. Since

\displaystyle\left\lVert n^{-1/2}\mathbf{X}_{0,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{p}),

\displaystyle\left\lVert n^{-1/2}(\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ})_{i=1}^{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle\left\lVert n^{-1/2}\mathbf{R}_{\eta,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\left\lVert\bm{\eta}_{i,n}\right\rVert^{2}\right\}^{1/2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

where
\bm{\eta}_{i,n}=\bm{r}_{i,n}-\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ}
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{L}_{n}-\mathbf{L}_{n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\lVert n^{-1/2}\mathbf{X}_{0,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert n^{-1/2}\mathbf{R}_{\eta,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\quad+2\left\lVert n^{-1/2}(\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ})_{i=1}^{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert n^{-1/2}\mathbf{R}_{\eta,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\lVert n^{-1/2}\mathbf{R}_{\eta,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

(D.12)

Consequently, (
D.12
) gives

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{F,n}-\mathbf{A}_{F,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\lVert\mathbf{V}_{F,n}-\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

(D.13)

Conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
, write

\bm{d}_{L,n}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}s_{j}\bm{a}_{j,n},\qquad\bm{d}_{0,n}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}s_{j}\bm{b}_{j,n},\qquad\bm{b}_{j,n}=\frac{\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{j}}{ne_{n}}.

On the event in (
C.13
) and (
C.14
),

\displaystyle\max_{j}\{\left\lVert\bm{a}_{j,n}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\bm{b}_{j,n}\right\rVert\}

\displaystyle=O(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{a,n}:=\sum_{j}\bm{a}_{j,n}\bm{a}_{j,n}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1},

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{b,n}:=\sum_{j}\bm{b}_{j,n}\bm{b}_{j,n}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{ne_{n}^{2}}\mathbf{B}_{n},

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{S}_{a,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\lVert\mathbf{S}_{b,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{S}_{a,n})+\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{S}_{b,n})

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.14)

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
be the bilinear map in
(
C.34
) with
\bm{U}_{i}
replaced by

\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{i}
. Equation (
D.10
) becomes

\displaystyle\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}s_{j}\bm{c}_{ij,n}+\frac{w_{i}^{2}}{2\sqrt{p}}s_{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{i}(\bm{d}_{L,n},\bm{d}_{L,n}),

\displaystyle\bm{c}_{ij,n}={}

\displaystyle-w_{i}(\bm{a}_{j,n}-\bm{b}_{j,n})+w_{i}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{i}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{i}^{\top}\bm{a}_{j,n}.

(D.15)

Thus
\mathbf{A}_{F,n}^{\circ}
and
\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\circ}
are exact Walsh
polynomials of degree at most six. If

\mathbf{A}_{F,n}^{\circ}=\sum_{|\mathcal{I}|\leq 6}s_{\mathcal{I}}\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{I},n}^{A},\qquad\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\circ}=\sum_{|\mathcal{I}|\leq 6}s_{\mathcal{I}}\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{I},n}^{V},

then Walsh orthogonality gives

\mathbb{E}_{s}\{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{F,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\lVert\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}\}=\sum_{\mathcal{I}}\{\left\lVert\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{I},n}^{A}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\lVert\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{I},n}^{V}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\}.

(D.16)

The largest coefficient classes are generated by the first two terms
of
\bm{c}_{ij,n}
. For the factor–factor block,

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i}(\bm{a}_{j,n}-\bm{b}_{j,n})^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\right\}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}(\bm{a}_{j,n}-\bm{b}_{j,n})\right\rVert^{2}\right\}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p)O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle\frac{Cp}{n^{2}}\sum_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\bm{U}_{i}\right\rVert^{4}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{a,n}\bm{U}_{i}\leq\frac{Cp}{n}\left\lVert\mathbf{S}_{a,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

For the two bulk–factor orientations, the corresponding sums are
bounded by

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{Y}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\right\}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}(\bm{a}_{j,n}-\bm{b}_{j,n})\right\rVert^{2}\right\}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle\frac{Cp}{n^{2}}\sum_{i}\left\lVert\bm{U}_{B,i}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\bm{U}_{F,i}\right\rVert^{2}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{a,n}\bm{U}_{i}\leq\frac{Cp}{n}\left\lVert\mathbf{S}_{a,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle\frac{Cp}{n^{2}}\sum_{i}\left\lVert\bm{U}_{F,i}\right\rVert^{2}\left\{\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{a,n}\bm{U}_{i}+\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{b,n}\bm{U}_{i}\right\}\leq C.

The Hessian coefficient arrays satisfy, by

\left\lVert\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{i}(\bm{a},\bm{b})\right\rVert\leq 6\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert\left\lVert\bm{b}\right\rVert
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}w_{i}^{2}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{i}\{\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{i}(\bm{a}_{j,n},\bm{a}_{k,n})\}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sum_{j}\left\lVert\bm{a}_{j,n}\right\rVert^{2}\right)^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}w_{i}^{2}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{i}\{\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{i}(\bm{a}_{j,n},\bm{a}_{k,n})\}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C.

The three summands in (
D.8
) are products of

\mathbf{X}_{0,n}
, which is a degree-one Walsh polynomial, and

\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ}
, whose degree-one and degree-three coefficient
families are displayed in (
D.15
). The contractions in
(
D.14
) and the preceding displays bound the
coefficient energy of every such base family, both after the
factor–factor projection and after either bulk–factor projection.
Replacing an
\bm{a}_{j,n}
family by a
\bm{b}_{j,n}
family obeys the
same bounds. Lemma
C.4
, applied conditionally on

\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
, then controls the coefficient energy of the

\mathbf{X}_{0,n}(\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ})^{\top}
terms and, by
(
C.29
), that of

\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ}(\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ})^{\top}
. Since the number of
linear and Hessian contraction patterns is fixed, these bounds imply

\sum_{\mathcal{I}}\{\left\lVert\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{I},n}^{A}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\lVert\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{I},n}^{V}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.17)

Equations (
D.16
)–(
D.17
),
conditional Markov’s inequality, and fixed
r
yield

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{F,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\lVert\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

The conclusion follows from (
D.13
).
∎

Lemma D.3
.

Let

\mathbf{S}_{HF,n}=\mathbf{B}_{n}+\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}+\mathbf{F}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p}.

Under Assumption 1,
\mathbf{S}_{HF,n}
is invertible with
probability tending to one. On this event write

\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}=\mathbf{S}_{HF,n}^{-1}
. For every fixed

\rho\in[\rho_{0},\rho_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

(D.18)

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{a},\bm{b}\in\{\bm{Z}_{n},\bm{W}_{n},\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\}}\left\lvert\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{E}_{n}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{b}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\log p),

(D.19)

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{v}\in\{\bm{Z}_{n},\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\}}\left\lvert\bm{d}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{E}_{n}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{v}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\{g_{p}(\bm{d})\},

(D.20)

for every deterministic
\bm{d}
, where

g_{p}(\bm{d})^{2}=p^{-1}\|\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\bm{d}\|^{2}+\|\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{d}\|^{2}
. In particular, the second quantity is

o_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{1/2})
.

Proof.

Put

\bm{d}_{L,n}=\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n},\qquad\bm{d}_{0,n}=e_{n}^{-1}\overline{\bm{Y}}_{n},\qquad\alpha_{i,n}=\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{d}_{L,n},

and write

\bm{b}_{B,n}=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}(\bm{d}_{L,n}-\bm{d}_{0,n}),\qquad\bm{d}_{B,n}=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{d}_{L,n}.

Lemmas
C.3
and
C.5
imply

\left\lVert\bm{d}_{L,n}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\bm{d}_{0,n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\left\lVert\bm{b}_{B,n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),\qquad\max_{i}|\alpha_{i,n}|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.21)

Let
\mathbf{U}_{B,n}=(\bm{U}_{B,1},\ldots,\bm{U}_{B,n})
,

\bm{w}=(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})^{\top}
, and let
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{v}}
denote the
diagonal matrix with diagonal
\bm{v}
. Projecting
(
D.10
) onto the bulk subspace gives the exact matrix
factorization

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{B,n}^{\circ}

\displaystyle:=(\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{r}_{1,n}^{\circ},\ldots,\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{r}_{n,n}^{\circ})

\displaystyle=-\bm{b}_{B,n}\bm{w}^{\top}+\mathbf{U}_{B,n}\mathbf{D}_{\bm{w}\odot\bm{\alpha}_{n}}-p^{-1/2}\bm{d}_{B,n}(\bm{w}^{2}\odot\bm{\alpha}_{n})^{\top}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\left\lVert\bm{d}_{L,n}\right\rVert^{2}}{2\sqrt{p}}\mathbf{U}_{B,n}\mathbf{D}_{\bm{w}^{2}}+\frac{3}{2\sqrt{p}}\mathbf{U}_{B,n}\mathbf{D}_{\bm{w}^{2}\odot\bm{\alpha}_{n}^{2}},

(D.22)

where
\bm{\alpha}_{n}=(\alpha_{1,n},\ldots,\alpha_{n,n})^{\top}
and
powers are componentwise. By (
C.9
),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{U}_{B,n}\mathbf{D}_{\bm{w}\odot\bm{\alpha}_{n}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i}\alpha_{i,n}^{2}\left\|\sum_{i}w_{i}^{2}\bm{U}_{B,i}\bm{U}_{B,i}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n/p)=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle p^{-1/2}\left\lVert\bm{d}_{B,n}\right\rVert\left\lVert\bm{w}^{2}\odot\bm{\alpha}_{n}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq Cp^{-1/2}\left\lVert\bm{d}_{B,n}\right\rVert\sqrt{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle p^{-1/2}\left\lVert\mathbf{U}_{B,n}\mathbf{D}_{\bm{w}^{2}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\quad+p^{-1/2}\left\lVert\mathbf{U}_{B,n}\mathbf{D}_{\bm{w}^{2}\odot\bm{\alpha}_{n}^{2}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{b}_{B,n}\bm{w}^{\top}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\bm{b}_{B,n}\right\rVert\bar{w}\sqrt{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Hence

\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{B,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.23)

By (
D.11
),

\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}(\bm{r}_{i,n}-\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ})_{i=1}^{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\left\{\sum_{i}\left\lVert\bm{r}_{i,n}-\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert^{2}\right\}^{1/2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

so the exact bulk residual matrix
\mathbf{R}_{B,n}=(\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{r}_{i,n})_{i=1}^{n}

also satisfies

\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.24)

Let

\mathbf{X}_{B,n}=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}(\bm{Y}_{i}-w_{i}\bm{d}_{0,n})_{i=1}^{n}.

Equations (
C.41
) and (
D.21
) give

\left\lVert n^{-1/2}\mathbf{X}_{B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Combining (
D.24
) with the exact residual representation (
D.8
) yields

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\left\lVert\mathbf{X}_{B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\frac{1}{n}\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

which proves (
D.18
) without a matrix-chaos inequality.

For the bilinear bounds, let
\mathbf{E}_{n}^{\circ}
denote the matrix in
(
D.8
) with
\bm{r}_{i,n}
replaced by

\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ}
. Equations (
D.11
),
(
D.23
), and

\left\lVert n^{-1/2}\mathbf{X}_{B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
give

\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{n}-\mathbf{E}_{n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

(D.25)

Conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
,

\bm{d}_{L,n}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}s_{j}\bm{a}_{j,n},\qquad\bm{d}_{0,n}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}s_{j}\bm{b}_{j,n},\qquad\bm{b}_{j,n}=\frac{\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{j}}{ne_{n}},

and

\displaystyle\max_{j}(\left\lVert\bm{a}_{j,n}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\bm{b}_{j,n}\right\rVert)

\displaystyle\leq Cp^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\bm{a}_{j,n}\bm{a}_{j,n}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{J}_{n}^{-1},

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\bm{b}_{j,n}\bm{b}_{j,n}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{ne_{n}^{2}}\mathbf{B}_{n},

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{j}\bm{a}_{j,n}\bm{a}_{j,n}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\|\sum_{j}\bm{b}_{j,n}\bm{b}_{j,n}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\sum_{j}(\left\lVert\bm{a}_{j,n}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert\bm{b}_{j,n}\right\rVert^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C.

(D.26)

The coefficient bounds in (
D.26
), together with
(
D.15
), show that
\mathbf{E}_{n}^{\circ}
is an exact Walsh
polynomial of degree at most six. Put

\mathcal{V}_{n}=\{\bm{Z}_{n},\bm{W}_{n},\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\},\qquad\bm{v}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}\bm{v}_{i,n}\quad(\bm{v}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}).

After inserting the two external sign sums, every oracle bilinear form
is a scalar Walsh polynomial of degree at most eight. Walsh
orthogonality and repeated Cauchy–Schwarz give

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{a},\bm{b}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}\sum_{\mathcal{I}}|c_{\mathcal{I},n}(\bm{a},\bm{b})|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\log p)^{2},

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{v}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}\sum_{\mathcal{I}}|c_{\mathcal{I},n}(\bm{d},\bm{v})|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cg_{p}(\bm{d})^{2}.

(D.27)

For example, the largest linear contraction is bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{Cp}{n^{2}}\left\|\sum_{i}\bm{U}_{B,i}\bm{U}_{B,i}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\left\|\sum_{j}\bm{a}_{j,n}\bm{a}_{j,n}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\operatorname{tr}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{v}_{i,n}\bm{v}_{i,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\right)\leq C,

while a deterministic external vector uses

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}(\bm{d}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{U}_{B,i})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{p}\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{d}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{C}{p}g_{p}(\bm{d})^{2}.

Every summand obtained after inserting (
D.15
) is a
bounded deterministic contraction of one of the displayed linear
coefficient families, one Hessian family, or a product of two such
families. Lemma
C.4
converts the preceding
second-moment contractions into the full coefficient-energy bounds in
(
D.27
); the product estimate
(
C.29
) covers the two-residual terms.
There are only finitely many contraction patterns because the total
Walsh degree is at most eight. A Hessian occurrence contributes an
additional
p^{-1/2}
before squaring, so no omitted pattern exceeds the
two displayed bounds. Hence conditional Chebyshev’s inequality and
(
D.25
) yield

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{a},\bm{b}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}|\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{E}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{b}|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\log p),

(D.28)

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{v}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}|\bm{d}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{E}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{v}|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\{g_{p}(\bm{d})\}.

(D.29)

Since

\mathbf{S}_{HF,n}=\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{E}_{n}
,

\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{S}_{HF,n})\geq\rho_{0}-\|\mathbf{E}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq\rho_{0}/2

with probability tending to one. Therefore

\|\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.30)

The matrices
\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}
are inverses of

\mathbf{B}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p}
plus fixed-rank
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
perturbations.
Together with (
C.39
), Weyl’s inequality and Wedin’s
sin–theta theorem
(
Stewart and Sun 1990
, Theorem V.4.1, pp. 260–262)
give

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

For
\bm{v}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}
, write

\bm{v}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i}s_{i}\bm{v}_{i,n}
as above and define

\mathbf{B}_{v,n}=n^{-1}\sum_{i}\bm{v}_{i,n}\bm{v}_{i,n}^{\top}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s}\|\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{v}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{B}_{v,n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p})

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}),&\bm{v}=\bm{Z}_{n},\\
\bar{w}^{2}\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}),&\bm{v}=\bm{W}_{n},\\
\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}),&\bm{v}=\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}.\end{cases}

Since
\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\preceq\mathbf{Q}_{n}
,
(
C.40
) gives the first two bounds
O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1})
.
For the third, the block matrices in (
C.46
) give

\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

Hence

\max_{\bm{v}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}\|\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{v}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

(D.31)

The two-dimensional Woodbury identity and

\|\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}),\qquad\max_{\bm{v}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}\|\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{v}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{p})

transfer (
D.31
) to
\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}
. Next,

\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}-\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}=-\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{F}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}
and
(
D.9
) imply, with

X_{v,n}=\|\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{v}\|
,

X_{v,n}\leq O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1})X_{v,n}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1})O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{p})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})X_{v,n}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{v}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}\{\|\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\bm{v}\|+\|\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{v}\|\}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

(D.32)

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{v}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}\{\|\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\bm{v}\|+\|\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{v}\|\}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{p}).

For deterministic
\bm{d}
, (
C.46
) yields

\|\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{d}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}\{g_{p}(\bm{d})\},\qquad\|\bm{d}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}\{g_{p}(\bm{d})p^{-1/2}\}.

Moreover, conditional Rademacher second moments give

\|\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{d}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}\{g_{p}(\bm{d})n^{-1/2}\}.

The rank-two Woodbury identity and then the preceding resolvent identity
for
\mathbf{F}_{n}
consequently give

\|\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\bm{d}\|+\|\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{d}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}\{g_{p}(\bm{d})\},\qquad\|\bm{d}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}\{g_{p}(\bm{d})p^{-1/2}\}.

(D.33)

Let
\mathbf{Q}_{\widehat{R},n}=(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}
. The identity

\mathbf{Q}_{\widehat{R},n}=\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}-\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{E}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{\widehat{R},n}

gives

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{E}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{\widehat{R},n}\bm{b}={}

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{E}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{b}

\displaystyle-\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{E}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{E}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{\widehat{R},n}\bm{b}.

By (
D.18
), (
D.30
), and
(
D.33
), the second term is

O_{\mathbb{P}}(\log p)\quad\text{for }\bm{a},\bm{b}\in\mathcal{V}_{n},\qquad O_{\mathbb{P}}\{g_{p}(\bm{d})\log p/\sqrt{p}\}=o_{\mathbb{P}}\{g_{p}(\bm{d})\}

in the mixed case.

It remains to remove
\mathbf{F}_{n}
and then the rank-two update. From

\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}-\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}=-\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{F}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}
and

\mathbf{F}_{n}=\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\mathbf{A}_{F,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}+\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\top}+\mathbf{V}_{F,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top},

relations (
D.9
), (
D.32
), and
(
D.33
) yield

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{a},\bm{b}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}|\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{E}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{b}-\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{E}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\bm{b}|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\log p),

(D.34)

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{v}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}|\bm{d}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{E}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{v}-\bm{d}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{E}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\bm{v}|=O_{\mathbb{P}}\{g_{p}(\bm{d})\}.

(D.35)

For example,

\|\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{V}_{F,n}\|\|\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{E}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{b}\|\leq O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{p})O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

and every other factorized term has the same or a smaller order.

Finally,

\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}=\mathbf{Q}_{n}-\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{K}_{H,n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n},\qquad\mathbf{K}_{H,n}=(\mathbf{C}_{n}^{-1}+\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n})^{-1},

where
\|\mathbf{K}_{H,n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Expanding both occurrences
of
\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}
gives four terms. Equations
(
D.28
)–(
D.29
)
imply

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{v}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}\|\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{E}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{v}\|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\log p/\sqrt{n}),

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{E}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\log p/n),

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{v}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}\|\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{v}\|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{p}).

Thus all four random–random terms are
O_{\mathbb{P}}(\log p)
, while the
mixed terms are
O_{\mathbb{P}}\{g_{p}(\bm{d})\}
. Combining these bounds with
(
D.34
)–(
D.35
) proves
(
D.19
)–(
D.20
).
∎

Lemma D.4
.

Let

\mathbf{U}_{n}=(\mathbf{H}_{n},\mathbf{P}_{F,p},\mathbf{V}_{F,n})

and

\mathbf{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{fr}}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{C}_{n}&\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{A}_{F,n}&\mathbf{I}_{r}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{I}_{r}&\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}.

Then

\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}+\mathbf{F}_{n}=\mathbf{U}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{fr}}\mathbf{U}_{n}^{\top},

and

(\mathbf{B}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p}+\mathbf{U}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{fr}}\mathbf{U}_{n}^{\top})^{-1}=\mathbf{Q}_{n}-\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{U}_{n}\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{U}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n},

(D.36)

where

\mathbf{M}_{n}=\{(\mathbf{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{fr}})^{-1}+\mathbf{U}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{U}_{n}\}^{-1}

exists with probability tending to one and

\left\lVert\mathbf{M}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. If

\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}=(\mathbf{B}_{n}+\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1},

then

\max_{\bm{a},\bm{b}\in\{\bm{Z}_{n},\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\}}\left\lvert\bm{a}^{\top}(\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}-\mathbf{Q}_{H,n})\bm{b}\right\rvert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.37)

Proof.

The factorization follows from Lemma
D.2
. On the
event where
e_{n}
is bounded away from zero,

\mathbf{C}_{n}^{-1}
exists and has bounded norm. The inverse of the factor
block is explicit:

\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{A}_{F,n}&\mathbf{I}_{r}\\
\mathbf{I}_{r}&\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{I}_{r}\\
\mathbf{I}_{r}&-\mathbf{A}_{F,n}\end{pmatrix}.

Hence (
D.9
),
\left\lVert\mathbf{H}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, and

\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=1
imply

\left\lVert\mathbf{U}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\lVert\mathbf{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{fr}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\lVert(\mathbf{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{fr}})^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.38)

By (
D.30
), the matrix on the left-hand side of
(
D.36
) has a bounded inverse with probability tending
to one. The reverse Woodbury identity gives

\mathbf{M}_{n}=\mathbf{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{fr}}-\mathbf{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{fr}}\mathbf{U}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{U}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{fr}}.

(D.39)

Equations (
D.38
)–(
D.39
) prove

\left\lVert\mathbf{M}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. This argument is needed because

\mathbf{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{fr}}
is indefinite; positive definiteness of the final
ridge matrix alone does not directly bound the middle inverse.

It remains to prove (
D.37
). The two matrices

\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}^{-1}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}^{-1}
differ from

\mathbf{B}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p}
by fixed-rank matrices with operator norm

O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Weyl’s inequality and Wedin’s sin–theta theorem

(
Stewart and Sun 1990
, Theorem V.4.1, pp. 260–262)
, used exactly as in
Lemma
C.6
, therefore give

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\lVert\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

(D.40)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

The two-dimensional Woodbury formula for
\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}
, together with
conditional Rademacher second moments, yields

\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

(D.41)

Indeed,

\mathbb{E}_{s}\|\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{Z}_{n}\|^{2}\leq\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1})
;
the rank-two correction has the same order because

\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1})
and

\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{Z}_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{1/2})
.

Let

X_{n}=\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert.

The resolvent identity and

\mathbf{F}_{n}=\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\mathbf{A}_{F,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}+\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\top}+\mathbf{V}_{F,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}

give

X_{n}\leq O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1})X_{n}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1})\left\lVert\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})X_{n}.

Since

\left\lVert\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert\leq O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{p})
,

X_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

(D.42)

Finally,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}(\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}-\mathbf{Q}_{H,n})\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\quad=\left\lvert\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{F}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{F,n}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

where (
D.9
), (
D.41
),
(
D.42
), and

\left\lVert\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{p})
are used. Replacing either external

\bm{Z}_{n}
by
\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}
gives the same bounds because
(
C.49
)–(
C.50
) preserve the scaled
factor–bulk orders. This proves (
D.37
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
D.1
.

The exact decomposition (
D.1
) and the rank bound
(
D.2
) follow from (
D.6
)–(
D.7
)
and Lemma
D.2
.

The rank-two matrix
\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}
has operator norm

O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Lemma
D.1
proves directly that

\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}
exists and has bounded operator norm with probability tending
to one, without using Proposition
D.2
. Its inverse differs
from
\mathbf{B}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p}
by an
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
perturbation. The spike gap in
(
C.39
) and Wedin’s sin–theta theorem
(
Stewart and Sun 1990
, Theorem V.4.1, pp. 260–262)
therefore give

\left\lVert\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),\qquad\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}),

which proves (
D.3
).

Put

\bm{v}_{n}=\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n},\qquad\bm{r}_{n}=\bm{r}_{\theta,B,n}.

Theorem
C.1
gives

\widehat{\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\theta}_{0,p}=\frac{\bm{Z}_{n}}{e_{n}\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\bm{v}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\bm{r}_{n},\qquad\left\lVert\bm{r}_{n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

The Rademacher calculation in (
C.53
), the
rank-two Woodbury identity, (
D.37
), and
(
D.19
) imply

\displaystyle\left\lvert\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{v}_{n}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\bm{v}_{n}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{v}_{n}\right\rvert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1+\log p).

Moreover,

\displaystyle n\left\lvert\bm{r}_{n}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}(e_{n}^{-1}\bm{Z}_{n}/\sqrt{n}+\bm{v}_{n}/\sqrt{n})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq Cn\left\lVert\bm{r}_{n}\right\rVert\left\|e_{n}^{-1}\bm{Z}_{n}/\sqrt{n}+\bm{v}_{n}/\sqrt{n}\right\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle n\bm{r}_{n}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{r}_{n}

\displaystyle\leq Cn\left\lVert\bm{r}_{n}\right\rVert^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

Consequently,

T_{n}(\rho)=e_{n}^{-2}\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{Z}_{n}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(1+\log p).

(D.43)

Finally, (
D.37
), the resolvent identity, and
(
D.19
) give

\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{Z}_{n}=\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\bm{Z}_{n}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(1+\log p).

Substitution into (
D.43
) proves
(
D.4
). Since
p/n
is bounded away from zero
and infinity,
1+\log p=o(n^{1/2})
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
D.2
.

Let

\mathbf{G}_{n}=\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}=\begin{pmatrix}x_{n}&y_{n}\\
y_{n}&z_{n}\end{pmatrix}.

The two-dimensional Woodbury identity gives

\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}(\mathbf{B}_{n}+\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}\mathbf{H}_{n}=\mathbf{G}_{n}(\mathbf{I}_{2}+\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{n})^{-1}.

Direct inversion of the
2\times 2
matrix gives

\frac{1}{e_{n}^{2}}\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}(\mathbf{B}_{n}+\mathbf{H}_{n}\mathbf{C}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{Z}_{n}=n\frac{x_{n}}{e_{n}^{2}+t_{n}x_{n}-2e_{n}y_{n}+y_{n}^{2}-x_{n}z_{n}}.

Theorem
D.1
proves (
D.5
). Lemma
D.1
gives

L(x_{n},y_{n},z_{n},e_{n},t_{n})=D_{n}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
, and
Proposition
B.1
shows that
D_{n}
is bounded away from
zero with probability tending to one.
∎

Appendix E
Conditional quadratic-form central limit theorem and theoretical null law

For every nonzero vector
\bm{y}
, let
\mathfrak{o}(\bm{y})
denote the unique element of
\{\bm{y},-\bm{y}\}
whose first nonzero coordinate is positive. Define
