\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i}=\mathfrak{o}(\bm{Y}_{i}),\qquad s_{i}=\begin{cases}1,&\bm{Y}_{i}=\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i},\\
-1,&\bm{Y}_{i}=-\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i},\end{cases}\qquad\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}=\sigma\{w_{i},\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i}:1\leq i\leq n\}.

Lemma E.1
.

Under Assumption 1 and
H_{0}
, conditionally on

\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
, the variables
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
are independent and

\mathbb{P}(s_{i}=1\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})=\mathbb{P}(s_{i}=-1\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})=\frac{1}{2},\qquad 1\leq i\leq n.

Proof.

For one observation, the transformation

\bm{G}_{i}\mapsto-\bm{G}_{i}
preserves Gaussian probability, leaves

w_{i}=\xi_{i}(h_{i,p}/q_{i,p})^{1/2}
and

\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i}=\mathfrak{o}(\bm{Y}_{i})
unchanged, and replaces

s_{i}
by
-s_{i}
. Hence, for every bounded measurable function
g
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{g(w_{i},\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i})\mathbf{1}(s_{i}=1)\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\{g(w_{i},\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i})\mathbf{1}(s_{i}=-1)\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\{g(w_{i},\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i})\}.

This is exactly the identity

\mathbb{P}(s_{i}=1\mid w_{i},\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i})=1/2
almost surely.
For arbitrary
\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{n}\in\{-1,1\}
and bounded
measurable functions
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}
, independence across observations
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\{g_{i}(w_{i},\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i})\mathbf{1}(s_{i}=\epsilon_{i})\}

\displaystyle\qquad=2^{-n}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}g_{i}(w_{i},\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i})=2^{-n}\mathbb{E}\prod_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}(w_{i},\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i}).

A monotone-class argument therefore yields

\mathbb{P}(s_{1}=\epsilon_{1},\ldots,s_{n}=\epsilon_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})=2^{-n}

almost surely, proving the claim. Notice that no independence between

w_{i}
and
\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i}
is used.
∎

Put
\mathbf{S}_{n}=\operatorname{diag}(s_{1},\ldots,s_{n})
and

\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}=(\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{n}),\qquad\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p}\right)^{-1}\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}.

Then

\mathbf{A}_{n}=\mathbf{S}_{n}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n}\mathbf{S}_{n}.

Write
a_{ij}=(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n})_{ij}
. Since

0\preceq\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n}\preceq\mathbf{I}_{n}
,

0\leq a_{ii}\leq 1,\qquad\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{ij}^{2}=(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n}^{2})_{ii}\leq 1,\qquad\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}a_{ij}^{2}\leq n.

(E.1)

Recall the three scalar quadratic forms from Proposition
D.2
:

x_{n}=n^{-1}\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{Z}_{n},\quad y_{n}=n^{-1}\bm{Z}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{W}_{n},\quad z_{n}=n^{-1}\bm{W}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{W}_{n}.

The sign representation gives the exact identities

\displaystyle x_{n}-\kappa_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}s_{i}s_{j},

(E.2)

\displaystyle y_{n}-b_{1,n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}(w_{i}+w_{j})s_{i}s_{j},

\displaystyle z_{n}-b_{2,n}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}w_{i}w_{j}s_{i}s_{j}.

(E.3)

Lemma E.2
.

By Lemma
E.1
, conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
,

\mathbb{E}_{s}\left(\left.\begin{pmatrix}x_{n}\\
y_{n}\\
z_{n}\end{pmatrix}\right|\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}\right)=\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{n}\\
b_{1,n}\\
b_{2,n}\end{pmatrix},

and

n\operatorname{Cov}_{s}\left(\begin{pmatrix}x_{n}\\
y_{n}\\
z_{n}\end{pmatrix}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}\right)=\bm{\Gamma}_{n},

where
\bm{\Gamma}_{n}
is defined in (2.4).

Proof.

For distinct unordered pairs
\{i,j\}\neq\{k,\ell\}
,

\mathbb{E}_{s}(s_{i}s_{j}s_{k}s_{\ell})=0,\qquad i<j,\quad k<\ell.

Equations (
E.2
)–(
E.3
) therefore have conditional mean zero after centering, and only equal pairs contribute to their conditional covariances.

For the first variance,

\displaystyle n\operatorname{Var}_{s}(x_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}^{2}=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i\neq j}a_{ij}^{2}=2\psi_{00,n}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle n\operatorname{Cov}_{s}(x_{n},y_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}^{2}(w_{i}+w_{j})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i\neq j}a_{ij}^{2}w_{i}=2\psi_{01,n},

\displaystyle n\operatorname{Cov}_{s}(x_{n},z_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}^{2}w_{i}w_{j}=2\psi_{11,n}.

The variance of the second component is

\displaystyle n\operatorname{Var}_{s}(y_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}^{2}(w_{i}+w_{j})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\neq j}a_{ij}^{2}w_{i}^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\neq j}a_{ij}^{2}w_{i}w_{j}

\displaystyle=\psi_{02,n}+\psi_{11,n},

where
\psi_{20,n}=\psi_{02,n}
was used. The remaining two entries are

\displaystyle n\operatorname{Cov}_{s}(y_{n},z_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}^{2}(w_{i}^{2}w_{j}+w_{i}w_{j}^{2})=2\psi_{12,n},

\displaystyle n\operatorname{Var}_{s}(z_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}^{2}w_{i}^{2}w_{j}^{2}=2\psi_{22,n}.

These six expressions are precisely the entries of
\bm{\Gamma}_{n}
.
∎

Theorem E.1
.

Under Assumption 1 and
H_{0}
, conditionally on

\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
,

\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}x_{n}-\kappa_{n}\\
y_{n}-b_{1,n}\\
z_{n}-b_{2,n}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N(\mathbf{0},\bm{\Gamma}_{n})

(E.4)

in probability, in the following triangular-array sense: for every bounded sequence of
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
-measurable vectors
\bm{c}_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\sqrt{n}\,\bm{c}_{n}^{\top}\begin{pmatrix}x_{n}-\kappa_{n}\\
y_{n}-b_{1,n}\\
z_{n}-b_{2,n}\end{pmatrix}\Big/(\bm{c}_{n}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{n}\bm{c}_{n})^{1/2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N(0,1)

whenever the denominator is bounded away from zero; if the denominator converges to zero, the unstandardized projection converges to zero in probability.

Proof.

For a bounded
\bm{c}_{n}=(c_{1,n},c_{2,n},c_{3,n})^{\top}
, define

h_{ij,n}=2c_{1,n}+c_{2,n}(w_{i}+w_{j})+2c_{3,n}w_{i}w_{j}.

Equations (
E.2
)–(
E.3
) give

\sqrt{n}\,\bm{c}_{n}^{\top}\begin{pmatrix}x_{n}-\kappa_{n}\\
y_{n}-b_{1,n}\\
z_{n}-b_{2,n}\end{pmatrix}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij}h_{ij,n}s_{i}s_{j}.

(E.5)

Because
0<w_{i}\leq\bar{w}
and
\|\bm{c}_{n}\|\leq C
,

|h_{ij,n}|\leq C
. Let
\mathbf{C}_{n}
be the symmetric matrix with zero diagonal and off-diagonal entries

(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}=\frac{a_{ij}h_{ij,n}}{2\sqrt{n}}.

Then the right-hand side of (
E.5
) is

\bm{s}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{n}\bm{s}
. The Hadamard multiplier
h_{ij,n}
has the finite decomposition

(h_{ij,n}a_{ij})_{ij}=2c_{1,n}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n}+c_{2,n}(\mathbf{D}_{w}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n}+\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{w})+2c_{3,n}\mathbf{D}_{w}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n}\mathbf{D}_{w},

up to its diagonal, where
\mathbf{D}_{w}=\operatorname{diag}(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})
. Therefore

\|\mathbf{C}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq Cn^{-1/2}.

(E.6)

Furthermore, by (
E.1
),

\max_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{n}\max_{i}\sum_{j}a_{ij}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{n},

and

\sum_{i,j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{4}\leq\left\{\max_{i}\sum_{j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{2}\right\}\sum_{i,j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}).

(E.7)

Put

V_{n}=\operatorname{Var}_{s}(\bm{s}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{n}\bm{s}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})=2\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{C}_{n}^{2})=\bm{c}_{n}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{n}\bm{c}_{n}.

To verify the fourth-moment requirement rather than merely citing it,
expand
\mathbb{E}_{s}(\bm{s}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{n}\bm{s})^{4}
. The terms whose four edges
split into two disjoint pairs give
3V_{n}^{2}
. Every remaining nonzero
term has a connected index graph. Grouping the connected graphs
according to whether they contain a four-cycle, two edges sharing a
vertex, or a repeated edge gives

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{s}(\bm{s}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{n}\bm{s})^{4}-3V_{n}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{C}_{n}^{4})+C\sum_{i}\left\{\sum_{j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{2}\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\sum_{i,j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\lVert\mathbf{C}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{C}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+C\left\{\max_{i}\sum_{j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{2}\right\}\left\lVert\mathbf{C}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\sum_{i,j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{4}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}).

The last equality follows from
(
E.6
)–(
E.7
) and

\left\lVert\mathbf{C}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=V_{n}/2=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Moreover, the maximal conditional influence satisfies

\max_{i}\sum_{j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{2}/V_{n}\leq Cn^{-1}/V_{n}.

Fix
\eta>0
. On
\{V_{n}\geq\eta\}
,

\frac{\max_{i}\sum_{j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{2}}{V_{n}}\leq\frac{C}{n\eta},\qquad\left|\frac{\mathbb{E}_{s}(\bm{s}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{n}\bm{s})^{4}}{V_{n}^{2}}-3\right|\leq\frac{C}{n\eta^{2}}.

Theorem 2.1 of
de Jong 1987
therefore applies uniformly on this
event. The asserted uniformity follows by contradiction: otherwise one
could select a deterministic sequence of coefficient arrays satisfying
the two displayed bounds for which the Gaussian approximation failed,
contradicting that theorem. On
\{V_{n}<\eta\}
, conditional
Cauchy–Schwarz gives

\mathbb{E}_{s}|\bm{s}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{n}\bm{s}|\leq\sqrt{\eta},\qquad\mathbb{E}|N(0,V_{n})|\leq\sqrt{\eta}.

Hence the conditional bounded-Lipschitz distance between the quadratic
form and
N(0,V_{n})
is at most
C\sqrt{\eta}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Letting first

n\to\infty
and then
\eta\downarrow 0
proves the conditional Gaussian
approximation without requiring
V_{n}
itself to converge. In
particular, standardization is valid whenever
V_{n}
is bounded away from
zero, while the unstandardized projection converges to zero whenever

V_{n}\to 0
. This proves every conditional Cramér–Wold projection and
therefore (
E.4
).
∎

Lemma E.3
.

Let
\mathcal{G}_{n}
be any sigma-field and let
a_{n}>0
be deterministic. If

R_{n}/a_{n}\to 0
in probability, then, for every
\varepsilon>0
,

\mathbb{P}\{|R_{n}|>\varepsilon a_{n}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0

in probability. Consequently, a conditionally asymptotically normal
statistic remains so after adding
R_{n}/a_{n}
.

Proof.

Put

P_{n}=\mathbb{P}\{|R_{n}|>\varepsilon a_{n}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\}
. Then
0\leq P_{n}\leq 1

and

\mathbb{E}P_{n}=\mathbb{P}(|R_{n}|>\varepsilon a_{n})\to 0.

For every
\delta>0
, Markov’s inequality gives

\mathbb{P}(P_{n}>\delta)\leq\mathbb{E}P_{n}/\delta\to 0
. The final assertion follows
from the conditional bounded-Lipschitz inequality

\left|\mathbb{E}\{f(X_{n}+R_{n}/a_{n})-f(X_{n})\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\}\right|\leq\varepsilon+2\|f\|_{\infty}\mathbb{P}\{|R_{n}|>\varepsilon a_{n}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\}

for every function
f
with Lipschitz constant at most one, followed by

\varepsilon\downarrow 0
.
∎

Proof of Theorem 2.1.

Let

\bm{q}_{n}=(x_{n},y_{n},z_{n})^{\top},\qquad\bm{q}_{0,n}=(\kappa_{n},b_{1,n},b_{2,n})^{\top}.

For fixed
e_{n},t_{n}
, define

\varphi_{n}(x,y,z)=\frac{x}{e_{n}^{2}+t_{n}x-2e_{n}y+y^{2}-xz}.

At
\bm{q}_{0,n}
, the denominator equals

\displaystyle e_{n}^{2}+t_{n}\kappa_{n}-2e_{n}b_{1,n}+b_{1,n}^{2}-\kappa_{n}b_{2,n}

\displaystyle\qquad=(e_{n}-b_{1,n})^{2}+\kappa_{n}(t_{n}-b_{2,n})=D_{n},

and

\nabla\varphi_{n}(\bm{q}_{0,n})=D_{n}^{-2}\begin{pmatrix}(e_{n}-b_{1,n})^{2}\\
2\kappa_{n}(e_{n}-b_{1,n})\\
\kappa_{n}^{2}\end{pmatrix}=\bm{g}_{n}.

Lemma
E.2
and
(
E.1
) give

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s}\{n\|\bm{q}_{n}-\bm{q}_{0,n}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}\}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}(\bm{\Gamma}_{n})\leq C,

\displaystyle\|\bm{q}_{n}-\bm{q}_{0,n}\|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

Let

\bm{q}_{n}(t)=\bm{q}_{0,n}+t(\bm{q}_{n}-\bm{q}_{0,n}),\qquad 0\leq t\leq 1.

On the event in (
B.18
), the five arguments are
bounded and
D_{n}\geq C_{1}
. Since
L(\cdot,e_{n},t_{n})
is a polynomial,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}|L(\bm{q}_{n}(t),e_{n},t_{n})-D_{n}|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\bm{q}_{n}-\bm{q}_{0,n}\|+C\|\bm{q}_{n}-\bm{q}_{0,n}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

Consequently,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\inf_{0\leq t\leq 1}L(\bm{q}_{n}(t),e_{n},t_{n})\geq C_{1}/2\right\}\to 1.

Every second derivative of
\varphi_{n}
is therefore uniformly bounded
along the complete line segment. Taylor’s theorem now yields

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}(\bm{q}_{n})-\varphi_{n}(\bm{q}_{0,n})

\displaystyle=\bm{g}_{n}^{\top}(\bm{q}_{n}-\bm{q}_{0,n})+R_{\varphi,n},

\displaystyle|R_{\varphi,n}|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\bm{q}_{n}-\bm{q}_{0,n}\|^{2},\qquad\sqrt{n}R_{\varphi,n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

By definition,

\varphi_{n}(\bm{q}_{0,n})=\kappa_{n}/D_{n}=\mu_{n}
and

\bm{g}_{n}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{n}\bm{g}_{n}=\sigma_{D,n}^{2}
. Theorem
E.1
, applied with the
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
-measurable vector
\bm{g}_{n}
, together with the preceding Taylor bound, yields

\frac{\sqrt{n}\{\varphi_{n}(\bm{q}_{n})-\mu_{n}\}}{\sigma_{D,n}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N(0,1)

(E.8)

conditionally in probability.

Proposition
D.2
gives

T_{n}(\rho)=n\varphi_{n}(\bm{q}_{n})+R_{T,n},\qquad R_{T,n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1+\log p).

Since
1+\log p=o(\sqrt{n})
and
\sigma_{D,n}^{2}
is bounded away from
zero,
R_{T,n}/\sqrt{n\sigma_{D,n}^{2}}\to 0
in probability.
Lemma
E.3
, with

\mathcal{G}_{n}=\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
, upgrades this unconditional rate to conditional
negligibility. Conditional Slutsky’s theorem and
(
E.8
) therefore prove pointwise convergence of
the conditional distribution functions. Since the limiting Gaussian
distribution is continuous, the conditional Pólya argument upgrades
pointwise convergence to the supremum in (2.6).
The unconditional convergence follows by taking expectations of bounded
continuous test functions. The order and non-degeneracy assertions follow from
(
B.18
).
∎

Appendix F
Feasible estimators and proof of the feasible null law

Let
\bm{\delta}_{n}=\widehat{\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\theta}_{0,p}
. Theorem
C.1
gives

\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Since

\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{\theta}_{0,p}=\sqrt{p}\,w_{i}^{-1}\bm{U}_{i}
under
H_{0}
,

\widehat{w}_{i}=w_{i}\left\{1-\frac{2w_{i}}{\sqrt{p}}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{n}+\frac{w_{i}^{2}}{p}\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|^{2}\right\}^{-1/2}.

(F.1)

Lemma F.1
.

Under Assumption 1 and
H_{0}
,

\displaystyle\max_{i\leq n}|\widehat{w}_{i}-w_{i}|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

(F.2)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{w}_{i}^{k}-w_{i}^{k})

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}),\qquad k=1,2.

(F.3)

Proof.

On the event
\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|\leq\sqrt{p}/(4\bar{w})
, whose probability tends to one, Taylor’s formula applied to (
F.1
) gives

\displaystyle\widehat{w}_{i}={}

\displaystyle w_{i}+\frac{w_{i}^{2}}{\sqrt{p}}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{n}+\frac{w_{i}^{3}}{2p}\{3(\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{n})^{2}-\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|^{2}\}+\mathcal{R}_{i,n},

(F.4)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}_{i,n}|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{w_{i}^{4}}{p^{3/2}}\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|^{3}.

Because
w_{i}\leq\bar{w}
,
\|\bm{U}_{i}\|=1
, and

\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, the maximum of the first correction is

O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})
and the remaining corrections are smaller. This proves (
F.2
).

For every fixed integer
a\geq 1
, central symmetry gives

\mathbb{E}(w_{i}^{a}\bm{U}_{i})=\mathbf{0}
. Therefore

\mathbb{E}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}^{a}\bm{U}_{i}\right\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}(w_{i}^{2a})\leq\frac{\bar{w}^{2a}}{n},

(F.5)

so the vector average is
O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
. Averaging
(
F.4
) and using
(
F.5
) yields

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n\sqrt{p}}\sum_{i}w_{i}^{2}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{n}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|}{\sqrt{p}}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}w_{i}^{2}\bm{U}_{i}\right\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}\{(np)^{-1/2}\}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{np}\sum_{i}w_{i}^{3}\{3(\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{n})^{2}+\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|^{2}\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\bar{w}^{3}}{p}\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}|\mathcal{R}_{i,n}|

\displaystyle\leq Cp^{-3/2}\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|^{3}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-3/2}).

This proves (
F.3
) for
k=1
. Squaring
(
F.4
) gives

\widehat{w}_{i}^{2}=w_{i}^{2}+\frac{2w_{i}^{3}}{\sqrt{p}}\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{n}+\frac{w_{i}^{4}}{p}\{4(\bm{U}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{n})^{2}-\|\bm{\delta}_{n}\|^{2}\}+\mathcal{R}_{i,n}^{(2)},

where
n^{-1}\sum_{i}|\mathcal{R}_{i,n}^{(2)}|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-3/2})
.
The same three bounds, with
w_{i}^{2}
replaced by
w_{i}^{3}
or
w_{i}^{4}
, prove the result for
k=2
.
∎

Let

\mathbf{K}_{n}=n^{-1}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\mathbf{Y},\qquad\widehat{\mathbf{K}}_{n}=n^{-1}\widehat{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Y}}.

Then

\mathbf{A}_{n}=\mathbf{K}_{n}(\mathbf{K}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1},\qquad\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}=\widehat{\mathbf{K}}_{n}(\widehat{\mathbf{K}}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}.

Lemma F.2
.

Under Assumption 1 and
H_{0}
, for every fixed

\rho\in[\rho_{0},\rho_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}-\mathbf{A}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(F.6)

\displaystyle\left|\operatorname{tr}\{\mathbf{D}_{w}^{k}(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}-\mathbf{A}_{n})\}\right|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad k=0,1,2,

(F.7)

where
\mathbf{D}_{w}=\operatorname{diag}(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\widehat{\kappa}_{n}-\kappa_{n}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}),

(F.8)

\displaystyle\widehat{b}_{a,n}-b_{a,n}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}),\qquad a=1,2,

(F.9)

\displaystyle\max_{a,b\in\{0,1,2\}}|\widehat{\psi}_{ab,n}-\psi_{ab,n}|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

(F.10)

Proof.

Let

\bm{w}=(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})^{\top},\qquad\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,n}=(\bm{r}_{1,n},\ldots,\bm{r}_{n,n}),\qquad\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,n}.

Since
\widehat{\mathbf{Y}}=\mathbf{Y}-\bm{d}_{0,n}\bm{w}^{\top}+\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,n}
, put

\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{fr},n}=-\bm{d}_{0,n}\bm{w}^{\top}+\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,n},\qquad\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}=\mathbf{Y}+\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{fr},n},\qquad\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{fr},n}=n^{-1}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}.

Then

\widehat{\mathbf{Y}}=\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}+\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}

and

\widehat{\mathbf{K}}_{n}-\mathbf{K}_{n}=\{\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{fr},n}-\mathbf{K}_{n}\}+\mathbf{E}_{K,n}.

where

\mathbf{E}_{K,n}=\frac{1}{n}\left(\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}+\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}+\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}\right).

(F.11)

Since
\operatorname{rank}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{fr},n})\leq r+1
,

\operatorname{rank}(\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{fr},n}-\mathbf{K}_{n})\leq 2r+2.

(F.12)

No operator-norm bound on this finite-rank Gram perturbation is needed;
along a pervasive sample direction its norm need not be
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

The factor part of
\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}
is orthogonal to

\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}
, so

\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}=\{\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}\}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\|n^{-1/2}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\|n^{-1/2}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{Y}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{d}_{0,n}\|\,\|\bm{w}\|/\sqrt{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle n^{-1}\|\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}\sum_{i}\|\bm{r}_{i,n}\|^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Thus

\|\mathbf{E}_{K,n}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{2}{n}\|\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\frac{1}{n}\|\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(F.13)

For the operator norm, let
\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,n}^{\circ}
have columns

\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ}
from (
D.10
), and put

\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}^{\circ}=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,n}^{\circ}
.
Equation (
D.22
) and
(
D.23
) give

\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Moreover, (
D.11
) gives

\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}-\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,n}-\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

and therefore

\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(F.14)

Substituting the operator bound (
F.14
) into (
F.11
) yields

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{K,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\frac{1}{n}\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

(F.15)

Define

\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{fr},n}=\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{fr},n}(\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{fr},n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}.

Both
\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{fr},n}
and
\mathbf{A}_{n}
are positive-semidefinite
contractions. Equations (
F.12
) and the
resolvent identity imply

\operatorname{rank}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{fr},n}-\mathbf{A}_{n})\leq 2r+2,\qquad\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{fr},n}-\mathbf{A}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1,\qquad\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{fr},n}-\mathbf{A}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{2r+2}.

Moreover,

\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}-\mathbf{A}_{\mathrm{fr},n}=\rho(\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{fr},n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\mathbf{E}_{K,n}(\widehat{\mathbf{K}}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}.

Thus (
F.13
) gives

\left\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}-\mathbf{A}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{2r+2}+\rho_{1}\rho_{0}^{-2}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{K,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

which proves (
F.6
).

For the weighted trace, put

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{K,n}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{K}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1},

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{E,n}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{K}_{n}+\mathbf{E}_{K,n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{K,n}

\displaystyle=(\widehat{\mathbf{K}}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}.

By (
F.15
),
\mathbf{R}_{E,n}
exists with probability tending
to one and all three resolvents have bounded operator norm. Since

\widehat{\mathbf{K}}_{n}-(\mathbf{K}_{n}+\mathbf{E}_{K,n})=\mathbf{K}_{\mathrm{fr},n}-\mathbf{K}_{n}
has rank at
most
2r+2
,

\displaystyle\left|\operatorname{tr}\{\mathbf{D}_{w}^{k}(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}-\mathbf{A}_{n})\}-\rho\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{D}_{w}^{k}\mathbf{R}_{K,n}\mathbf{E}_{K,n}\mathbf{R}_{E,n})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\rho\left|\operatorname{tr}\{\mathbf{D}_{w}^{k}(\mathbf{R}_{E,n}-\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{K,n})\}\right|\leq C(2r+2)\bar{w}^{k}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

The second resolvent identity gives

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{D}_{w}^{k}\mathbf{R}_{K,n}\mathbf{E}_{K,n}\mathbf{R}_{E,n})={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{D}_{w}^{k}\mathbf{R}_{K,n}\mathbf{E}_{K,n}\mathbf{R}_{K,n})

\displaystyle-\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{D}_{w}^{k}\mathbf{R}_{K,n}\mathbf{E}_{K,n}\mathbf{R}_{K,n}\mathbf{E}_{K,n}\mathbf{R}_{E,n}).

(F.16)

The second term is

O_{\mathbb{P}}\{n\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{K,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

To handle the first term, define

\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{fr},n}^{\circ}=-\bm{d}_{0,n}\bm{w}^{\top}+\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,n}^{\circ},\qquad\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}^{\circ}=\mathbf{Y}+\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{fr},n}^{\circ},

and let
\mathbf{E}_{K,n}^{\circ}
be the matrix in (
F.11
) with

\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}
and
\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}
replaced by

\mathbf{Y}_{\mathrm{fr},n}^{\circ}
and

\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}^{\circ}
. Put

\mathbf{R}_{\eta B,n}=\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}-\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}^{\circ},\qquad\mathbf{X}_{B,n}=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}(\mathbf{Y}-\bm{d}_{0,n}\bm{w}^{\top}).

Bulk–factor orthogonality gives

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{K,n}-\mathbf{E}_{K,n}^{\circ}=\frac{1}{n}\{

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{B,n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{\eta B,n}+\mathbf{R}_{\eta B,n}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{B,n}+(\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{\eta B,n}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{R}_{\eta B,n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}^{\circ}+\mathbf{R}_{\eta B,n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{\eta B,n}\}.

By (
D.11
),
(
D.23
), and

\left\lVert n^{-1/2}\mathbf{X}_{B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{K,n}-\mathbf{E}_{K,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}\left\lVert\mathbf{X}_{B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{\eta B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}+\frac{2}{n}\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{\eta B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}+\frac{1}{n}\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{\eta B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

(F.17)

Consequently,

\left|\operatorname{tr}\{\mathbf{D}_{w}^{k}\mathbf{R}_{K,n}(\mathbf{E}_{K,n}-\mathbf{E}_{K,n}^{\circ})\mathbf{R}_{K,n}\}\right|\leq C\sqrt{n}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{K,n}-\mathbf{E}_{K,n}^{\circ}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

Write
\mathbf{K}_{n}=\mathbf{S}_{n}\widetilde{\mathbf{K}}_{n}\mathbf{S}_{n}
and

\mathbf{R}_{K,n}=\mathbf{S}_{n}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{K,n}\mathbf{S}_{n}
, and set

\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}_{k,n}=\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{K,n}\mathbf{D}_{w}^{k}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{K,n},\qquad\mathbf{M}_{k,n}=\mathbf{R}_{K,n}\mathbf{D}_{w}^{k}\mathbf{R}_{K,n}=\mathbf{S}_{n}\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}_{k,n}\mathbf{S}_{n}.

Then

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}_{k,n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C,\qquad\max_{i}\sum_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}_{k,n})_{ij}^{2}\leq C,\qquad\operatorname{tr}(\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}_{k,n}^{2})\leq Cn.

Bulk–factor orthogonality in (
F.11
) gives the exact
identity

\displaystyle T_{k,n}^{\circ}

\displaystyle:={\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{D}_{w}^{k}\mathbf{R}_{K,n}\mathbf{E}_{K,n}^{\circ}\mathbf{R}_{K,n})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\operatorname{tr}\{\mathbf{M}_{k,n}\mathbf{X}_{B,n}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}^{\circ}\}+\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}\{\mathbf{M}_{k,n}(\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}^{\circ})^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}^{\circ}\}

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{C}_{k,n}+\mathcal{Q}_{k,n}.

The quadratic residual term does not require cancellation. Indeed,

|\mathcal{Q}_{k,n}|\leq\frac{C}{n}\|\mathbf{R}_{\Delta,B,n}^{\circ}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

because
n^{-1}\sum_{i}\|\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ}\|^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
We now treat the cross term and include its constant Walsh coefficient
explicitly.

Conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
, put

\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{B,i}=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i},\qquad\bm{a}_{B,j}=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{a}_{j,n},\qquad\bm{b}_{B,j}=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{b}_{j,n}.

Then

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{B,i}

\displaystyle=s_{i}\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{B,i}-w_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{n}s_{j}\bm{b}_{B,j},

\displaystyle\bm{r}_{B,i}^{\circ}

\displaystyle:=\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{r}_{i,n}^{\circ}=\bm{r}_{B,i}^{L}+\bm{r}_{B,i}^{H},

\displaystyle\bm{r}_{B,i}^{L}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}s_{j}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{c}_{ij,n},

\displaystyle\bm{r}_{B,i}^{H}

\displaystyle=\frac{w_{i}^{2}}{2\sqrt{p}}s_{i}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{i}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}s_{j}\bm{a}_{j,n},\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}s_{\ell}\bm{a}_{\ell,n}\right),

where
\bm{c}_{ij,n}
is defined in (
D.15
). Hence

\mathcal{C}_{k,n}=\mathcal{C}_{k,n}^{L}+\mathcal{C}_{k,n}^{H}
is a Walsh polynomial of degree at most six.
Write

\mathcal{C}_{k,n}^{L}=\sum_{\mathcal{I}}s_{\mathcal{I}}d_{\mathcal{I},n}^{L},\qquad\mathcal{C}_{k,n}^{H}=\sum_{\mathcal{I}}s_{\mathcal{I}}d_{\mathcal{I},n}^{H}.

Let

\mathbf{S}_{a,n}=\sum_{j}\bm{a}_{j,n}\bm{a}_{j,n}^{\top},\qquad\mathbf{S}_{b,n}=\sum_{j}\bm{b}_{j,n}\bm{b}_{j,n}^{\top}.

The bounds in (
D.14
) imply

\|\mathbf{S}_{a,n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|\mathbf{S}_{b,n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{S}_{a,n})+\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{S}_{b,n})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

After inserting the preceding linear representations and collecting
equal Walsh monomials, Cauchy–Schwarz gives, simultaneously for the
four choices generated by

\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{B,i}
or
\bm{b}_{B,j}
and by

\bm{a}_{j,n}-\bm{b}_{j,n}
or

\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{i}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{i}^{\top}\bm{a}_{j,n}
,

\displaystyle|d_{\varnothing,n}^{L}|^{2}+\sum_{\mathcal{I}\neq\varnothing}|d_{\mathcal{I},n}^{L}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{p}\left\{\max_{i}\sum_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}_{k,n})_{ij}^{2}\right\}\left\|\sum_{i}\bm{U}_{B,i}\bm{U}_{B,i}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\{\|\mathbf{S}_{a,n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|\mathbf{S}_{b,n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\}\operatorname{tr}(\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}_{k,n}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C}{n}\operatorname{tr}(\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}_{k,n}^{2})\{\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{S}_{a,n})+\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{S}_{b,n})\}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

This display includes the empty-set coefficient and therefore controls
the conditional mean, not only the conditional variance.

For the Hessian part, polarization

\|\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{i}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\|\leq 6\|\bm{u}\|\|\bm{v}\|
, the explicit factor
p^{-1/2}
, and
(
C.29
) yield

\displaystyle|d_{\varnothing,n}^{H}|^{2}+\sum_{\mathcal{I}\neq\varnothing}|d_{\mathcal{I},n}^{H}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{p}\left\{\max_{i}\sum_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}_{k,n})_{ij}^{2}\right\}\operatorname{tr}(\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}_{k,n}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\left\{1+\left\|\sum_{i}\bm{U}_{B,i}\bm{U}_{B,i}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right\}\{\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{S}_{a,n})\}^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Indeed, (
C.9
) gives

\|\sum_{i}\bm{U}_{B,i}\bm{U}_{B,i}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n/p)
,
while
\operatorname{tr}(\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}_{k,n}^{2})=O(n)
and
n/p=O(1)
.
The two displays exhaust the degree-one and Hessian families in
(
D.15
); the residual–residual product has already
been controlled by
\mathcal{Q}_{k,n}
. Walsh orthogonality now gives

|\mathbb{E}_{s}(\mathcal{C}_{k,n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\operatorname{Var}_{s}(\mathcal{C}_{k,n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

and hence
\mathcal{C}_{k,n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Thus

T_{k,n}^{\circ}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Together with
(
F.17
) and (
F.16
), this
proves (
F.7
).

For
a=1,2
,

\displaystyle\widehat{b}_{a,n}-b_{a,n}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}\{\mathbf{D}_{w}^{a}(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}-\mathbf{A}_{n})\}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{n})_{ii}(\widehat{w}_{i}^{a}-w_{i}^{a})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\{(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n})_{ii}-(\mathbf{A}_{n})_{ii}\}(\widehat{w}_{i}^{a}-w_{i}^{a}).

The first term is
O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1})
. The leading conditional
Rademacher part of the second is

L_{a,n}=\frac{a}{n\sqrt{p}}\sum_{i,j}a_{ii}w_{i}^{a+1}s_{i}s_{j}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{i}^{\top}\bm{a}_{j,n},\qquad a_{ii}=(\mathbf{A}_{n})_{ii},

and

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}_{s}(L_{a,n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a}{n\sqrt{p}}\sum_{i}|a_{ii}|w_{i}^{a+1}\|\bm{a}_{i,n}\|\leq Cp^{-1},

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}_{s}(L_{a,n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{-2}.

Thus
L_{a,n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1})
. The quadratic weight term and the
replacement remainder in Lemma
F.1
are also

O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1})
. Finally,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}|\{(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n})_{ii}-(\mathbf{A}_{n})_{ii}\}(\widehat{w}_{i}^{a}-w_{i}^{a})|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{n}\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}-\mathbf{A}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left\{\sum_{i}(\widehat{w}_{i}^{a}-w_{i}^{a})^{2}\right\}^{1/2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}).

This proves (
F.8
)–(
F.9
).

Put
\Delta_{A}=\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}-\mathbf{A}_{n}
. Since both companion matrices are
contractions,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\neq j}|(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n})_{ij}^{2}-(\mathbf{A}_{n})_{ij}^{2}|\leq\frac{1}{n}\|\Delta_{A}\|_{\mathrm{F}}(\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|\mathbf{A}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{F}})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

Together with (
F.2
) and

n^{-1}\sum_{i,j}(\mathbf{A}_{n})_{ij}^{2}\leq 1
, this proves
(
F.10
).
∎

Theorem F.1
.

Under Assumption 1 and
H_{0}
, for every fixed

\rho\in[\rho_{0},\rho_{1}]
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{e}_{n}-e_{n}|+|\widehat{t}_{n}-t_{n}|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}),

(F.18)

\displaystyle|\widehat{D}_{n}-D_{n}|+|\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\mu_{n}|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}),

(F.19)

\displaystyle|\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}^{2}-\sigma_{D,n}^{2}|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

(F.20)

Proof.

Equation (
F.18
) follows from Lemma
F.1
.
Lemma
F.2
, specifically
(
F.8
) and (
F.9
), together with
(
F.18
), shows that every argument of

D(e,t,k,b_{1},b_{2})=(e-b_{1})^{2}+k(t-b_{2})

is estimated with error
O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1})
. Proposition
B.1
places these arguments in a fixed compact set and bounds
D_{n}
away from zero. The mean-value theorem gives

\widehat{D}_{n}-D_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}),\qquad\frac{\widehat{\kappa}_{n}}{\widehat{D}_{n}}-\frac{\kappa_{n}}{D_{n}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}),

which proves (
F.19
).

Lemma
F.2
, specifically (
F.10
), shows that every entry of
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{n}-\bm{\Gamma}_{n}
is
O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
, and hence

\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{n}-\bm{\Gamma}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

The vector
\bm{g}(e,t,k,b_{1},b_{2})
is continuously differentiable on the same compact set, so

\|\widehat{\bm{g}}_{n}-\bm{g}_{n}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}).

Therefore

\displaystyle|\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}^{2}-\sigma_{D,n}^{2}|

\displaystyle\leq|(\widehat{\bm{g}}_{n}-\bm{g}_{n})^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{n}\widehat{\bm{g}}_{n}|

\displaystyle\quad+|\bm{g}_{n}^{\top}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{n}-\bm{\Gamma}_{n})\widehat{\bm{g}}_{n}|+|\bm{g}_{n}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{n}(\widehat{\bm{g}}_{n}-\bm{g}_{n})|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}),

which proves (
F.20
).
∎

Proof of Theorem 2.2.

By Theorem 2.1,

\frac{T_{n}(\rho)-n\mu_{n}}{\sqrt{n\sigma_{D,n}^{2}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N(0,1).

Theorem
F.1
gives

\frac{n(\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\mu_{n})}{\sqrt{n}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}^{2}}{\sigma_{D,n}^{2}}-1=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}),

where the second relation uses the positive lower bound in
(
B.18
). Hence

\displaystyle Z_{n}(\rho)

\displaystyle=\frac{T_{n}(\rho)-n\mu_{n}}{\sqrt{n\sigma_{D,n}^{2}}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{D,n}^{2}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}^{2}}\right)^{1/2}-\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\mu_{n})}{\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{T_{n}(\rho)-n\mu_{n}}{\sqrt{n\sigma_{D,n}^{2}}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Slutsky’s theorem proves (2.7). The positive lower and upper bounds for
\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}^{2}
follow from
Theorem
F.1
and Proposition
B.1
.
∎

Appendix G
Proofs under location alternatives

Under the shifted model (2.18), denote by

\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{\varepsilon,n}
the spatial median and the sample-median-centered spatial-sign covariance matrix computed from the noise observations
\sqrt{p}\,R_{i}\bm{\Omega}_{p}^{1/2}\bm{G}_{i}/\left\lVert\bm{G}_{i}\right\rVert
.

Proposition G.1
.

For every deterministic
\bm{\Delta}_{p}
,

\widehat{\bm{\theta}}=\bm{\theta}_{0,p}+\bm{\Delta}_{p}+\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon},\qquad\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}=\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{\varepsilon,n}.

The residual inverse radii
\widehat{w}_{i}
and all nuisance estimators in Section 2.3.2 have exactly the same distribution under (2.18) as under
H_{0}
.

Proof of Proposition
G.1
.

For every
\bm{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\lVert\bm{X}_{i}-\bm{t}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{0,p}+\bm{\Delta}_{p}+\sqrt{p}\,R_{i}\bm{\Omega}_{p}^{1/2}\bm{G}_{i}/\left\lVert\bm{G}_{i}\right\rVert-\bm{t}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\lVert\sqrt{p}\,R_{i}\bm{\Omega}_{p}^{1/2}\bm{G}_{i}/\left\lVert\bm{G}_{i}\right\rVert-\{\bm{t}-(\bm{\theta}_{0,p}+\bm{\Delta}_{p})\}\right\rVert.

The change of variable

\bm{s}=\bm{t}-(\bm{\theta}_{0,p}+\bm{\Delta}_{p})
proves

\widehat{\bm{\theta}}=\bm{\theta}_{0,p}+\bm{\Delta}_{p}+\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}.

Consequently,

\bm{X}_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\theta}}=\sqrt{p}\,R_{i}\bm{\Omega}_{p}^{1/2}\bm{G}_{i}/\left\lVert\bm{G}_{i}\right\rVert-\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon},

which proves
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{n}=\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{\varepsilon,n}
and the equality of all residual inverse-radius and angular nuisance estimators.
∎

Lemma G.1
.

Let

\mathbf{D}_{s,p}=\sqrt{p}\,\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}+\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}.

For the noise sample, with probability tending to one,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{\varepsilon,n}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq Cp,

(G.1)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{\varepsilon,n}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{p},

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{\varepsilon,n}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq C.

(G.2)

Hence

\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{\varepsilon,n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p}\preceq C\mathbf{D}_{s,p}^{2},

(G.3)

and

(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{\varepsilon,n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}\succeq c\left(p^{-1}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}+\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\right).

(G.4)

In particular, for every deterministic
\bm{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
,

\bm{a}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{\varepsilon,n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{a}\geq c\left\{p^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}\right\}.

(G.5)

Proof.

The oracle matrix satisfies

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{B}_{n}\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p),

(G.6)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\mathbf{B}_{n}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{p}),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\mathbf{B}_{n}\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(G.7)

The first relation follows from
\operatorname{tr}(\bm{\Pi}_{F}\mathbf{B}_{n}\bm{\Pi}_{F})\leq\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{B}_{n})=p
and fixed
r
. The third follows from the separable bulk proxy bounds in the proof of Proposition
B.1
. For the cross block,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F}\mathbf{B}_{n}\bm{\Pi}_{B}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F}\mathbf{B}_{n}\bm{\Pi}_{F}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B}\mathbf{B}_{n}\bm{\Pi}_{B}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p),

where the inequality follows from the positive-semidefinite block factorization
(
Bhatia 2007
, Proposition 1.3.2, pp. 13–14)
.
Theorem
D.1
and Lemmas
D.2
–
D.3
show that, after the
leading rank-two term is included, the remaining factor–factor and
factor–bulk blocks are
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, while the pure-bulk block is

O_{\mathbb{P}}\{(\log p/p)^{1/2}\}
. The rank-two term itself obeys the bounds in
(
G.1
)–(
G.2
) because

\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{p}),\quad\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{p}),\quad\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{n}\right\rVert/\sqrt{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

and the same relations hold for
\bm{W}_{n}
. This proves
(
G.1
)–(
G.2
).

For
\bm{x}=\bm{x}_{F}+\bm{x}_{B}
, the block bounds and
2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
give

\displaystyle\bm{x}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{\varepsilon,n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})\bm{x}

\displaystyle\leq Cp\left\lVert\bm{x}_{F}\right\rVert^{2}+2C\sqrt{p}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{F}\right\rVert\left\lVert\bm{x}_{B}\right\rVert+C\left\lVert\bm{x}_{B}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\{p\left\lVert\bm{x}_{F}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert\bm{x}_{B}\right\rVert^{2}\}=C_{1}\bm{x}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{s,p}^{2}\bm{x}.

This proves (
G.3
). Inversion reverses the Loewner order and gives (
G.4
); (
G.5
) follows by taking a quadratic form.
∎

Lemma G.2
.

Let

\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}=(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{\varepsilon,n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}

and let
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}
be the noise spatial median. For every deterministic triangular array
\bm{a}=\bm{a}_{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
,

\bm{a}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\left\{\frac{1}{n}\bm{a}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\bm{a}\right\}^{1/2}\right].

(G.8)

Proof.

Put

g_{a,p}^{2}=p^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{a}\right\rVert^{2},\qquad S_{a,n}=\bm{a}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\bm{a}.

Lemma
G.1
gives

g_{a,p}^{2}\leq CS_{a,n}.

(G.9)

The block orders in (
C.46
) imply

\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{a}\leq Cg_{a,p}^{2}
. Conditional on

\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}=\sigma\{w_{i},\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{i}:1\leq i\leq n\}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s}\{(\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{Z}_{n})^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}\}

\displaystyle=\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{a}\leq Cg_{a,p}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s}\{(\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{W}_{n})^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}\}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{w}^{2}\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{a}\leq Cg_{a,p}^{2}.

Thus

\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(g_{a,p}n^{-1/2}).

(G.10)

Let
\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}
be the rank-two resolvent in
Lemma
D.4
. Its two-dimensional middle inverse is
bounded because the denominator in Proposition
D.2
is
bounded away from zero. Since

\mathbf{H}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{Z}_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{1/2})
, the Woodbury identity and
(
G.10
) give

\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\bm{Z}_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(g_{a,p}).

(G.11)

The scaled block calculation in the proof of Theorem
C.1

also gives

\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(g_{a,p}).

For completeness, conditional second moments of the two uncorrected
terms are bounded by

\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{a}\leq Cg_{a,p}^{2},

which follows from (
C.47
),
(
C.48
), and
(
G.6
)–(
G.7
); the rank-two
correction is then treated exactly as in (
G.11
).

We next insert the factor correction. From
(
D.40
), (
D.9
), and the scaled
block bounds,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(g_{a,p}p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{V}_{F,n}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(g_{a,p}),

(G.12)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{v}_{n}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{v}_{n}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{1/2}),

for
\bm{v}_{n}\in\{\bm{Z}_{n},\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\}
. The second line follows
from the argument leading to (
D.42
); replacing

\bm{Z}_{n}
by
\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}
leaves the same scaled block orders.
Since every summand of

\mathbf{F}_{n}=\mathbf{P}_{F}\mathbf{A}_{F,n}\mathbf{P}_{F}^{\top}+\mathbf{P}_{F}\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\top}+\mathbf{V}_{F,n}\mathbf{P}_{F}^{\top}

contains at least one factor projection, the resolvent identity and
(
G.12
) yield

\bm{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{v}_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(g_{a,p}),\qquad\bm{v}_{n}\in\{\bm{Z}_{n},\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}\}.

The pure-bulk estimated-center remainder is handled by
(
D.20
); hence

\bm{a}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\bm{Z}_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(g_{a,p}),\qquad\bm{a}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(g_{a,p}).

(G.13)

Finally, Theorem
C.1
gives

\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{e_{n}\sqrt{n}}\bm{Z}_{n}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbf{G}_{F,n}\bm{Z}_{n}+\bm{r}_{\theta,B,n},\qquad\left\lVert\bm{r}_{\theta,B,n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

Since
\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{p}\,g_{a,p}
,

\left\lvert\bm{a}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\bm{r}_{\theta,B,n}\right\rvert\leq\rho_{0}^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{a}\right\rVert\left\lVert\bm{r}_{\theta,B,n}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(g_{a,p}p^{-1/2}).

Equation (
G.13
),
p/n\asymp 1
, and

e_{n}^{-1}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
therefore imply

\bm{a}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(g_{a,p}n^{-1/2}).

Combining this relation with (
G.9
) proves
(
G.8
).
∎

Lemma G.3
.

Let
\bm{d}_{p}
be deterministic,
\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\bm{d}_{p}=\mathbf{0}
, and
\left\lVert\bm{d}_{p}\right\rVert\leq C
. Let
(\delta_{p,\rho},\widetilde{\delta}_{p,\rho})
solve (
B.1
)–(
B.2
), and define

\mathbf{Q}_{B,p}^{\star}(\rho)=\mathbf{P}_{B,p}\{\rho(\mathbf{I}_{p-r}+\widetilde{\delta}_{p,\rho}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p})\}^{-1}\mathbf{P}_{B,p}^{\top}.

For the noise sample in (2.18), let

\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}=(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{\varepsilon,n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p})^{-1}.

Then, for every fixed
\rho\in[\rho_{0},\rho_{1}]
,

\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\bm{d}_{p}-\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{B,p}^{\star}(\rho)\bm{d}_{p}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{p}}\right).

(G.14)

If
\mathcal{G}_{p}\Rightarrow\mathcal{G}
as in (2.13), then

\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{B,p}^{\star}(\rho)\bm{d}_{p}\to q_{\rho}(\mathcal{G}).

(G.15)

Proof.

Write
\bm{d}_{B,p}=\mathbf{P}_{B,p}^{\top}\bm{d}_{p}
and

\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{B,n}=(\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{B,n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p-r})^{-1},

where
\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{B,n}
is defined in
(
B.19
). Conditional on

\mathbf{D}_{p}^{0}=\operatorname{diag}(D_{1,p}^{0},\ldots,D_{n,p}^{0})
, let

(\delta_{n,\rho}^{D},\widetilde{\delta}_{n,\rho}^{D})
solve

\displaystyle\delta_{n,\rho}^{D}

\displaystyle=c_{B,n}\int\frac{t}{\rho(1+\widetilde{\delta}_{n,\rho}^{D}t)}\,\,\mathrm{d}H_{B,p}(t),

(G.16)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}_{n,\rho}^{D}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{D_{i,p}^{0}}{\rho(1+\delta_{n,\rho}^{D}D_{i,p}^{0})}.

(G.17)

Define

\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{B,n}^{D}=\{\rho(\mathbf{I}_{p-r}+\widetilde{\delta}_{n,\rho}^{D}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p})\}^{-1}.

The bilinear resolvent estimate in Theorem 1.1 of

Hachem et al. 2013
, applied conditionally on

\mathbf{D}_{p}^{0}
, gives the explicit second-moment bound

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{G}_{B}}\left[\left|\bm{d}_{B,p}^{\top}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{B,n}-\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{B,n}^{D})\bm{d}_{B,p}\right|^{2}\middle|\mathbf{D}_{p}^{0}\right]\leq\frac{C}{p}.

The constant is uniform in
p
,
\rho
, and

\left\lVert\bm{d}_{B,p}\right\rVert\leq C
because

\omega_{-}\leq\lambda_{j,p}\leq\omega_{+},\qquad 0<D_{i,p}^{0}\leq\mu_{-}^{-1},\qquad\rho_{0}\leq\rho\leq\rho_{1}.

Consequently,

\bm{d}_{B,p}^{\top}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{B,n}-\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{B,n}^{D})\bm{d}_{B,p}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

(G.18)

We next replace the empirical column-scale equations
(
G.16
)–(
G.17
)
by their population versions
(
B.1
)–(
B.2
).
These are exactly the empirical and deterministic systems compared in
Lemma
B.2
. Hence, without a second
linearization argument,

|\delta_{n,\rho}^{D}-\delta_{p,\rho}|+|\widetilde{\delta}_{n,\rho}^{D}-\widetilde{\delta}_{p,\rho}|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

(G.19)

Since

\left|\frac{1}{\rho(1+x\lambda)}-\frac{1}{\rho(1+y\lambda)}\right|\leq\frac{\omega_{+}}{\rho_{0}}|x-y|,

combining this Lipschitz bound with (
G.19
), we obtain

\left\lVert\overline{\mathbf{T}}_{B,n}^{D}-\{\rho(\mathbf{I}_{p-r}+\widetilde{\delta}_{p,\rho}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p})\}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

(G.20)

Equations (
G.18
) and
(
G.20
) imply

\displaystyle\bm{d}_{B,p}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{B,n}\bm{d}_{B,p}-\bm{d}_{B,p}^{\top}\{\rho(\mathbf{I}_{p-r}+\widetilde{\delta}_{p,\rho}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p})\}^{-1}\bm{d}_{B,p}

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

(G.21)

Let

\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}=(\mathbf{P}_{B,p}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{B,p}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p-r})^{-1}.

Put

\mathbf{D}_{q}=\operatorname{diag}(q_{1,p}^{-1},\ldots,q_{n,p}^{-1}).

The
p
-dimensional bulk matrices satisfy

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{B,p}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{B,p}-\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{B,n}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}^{1/2}\mathbf{G}_{B}(\mathbf{D}_{q}-\mathbf{D}_{p}^{0})\mathbf{G}_{B}^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}^{1/2}.

Lemma
A.1
and the Gaussian operator-norm bound
(
Vershynin 2018
, Theorem 4.4.5, pp. 90–91)

give

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{D}_{q}-\mathbf{D}_{p}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{p}}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\lVert\mathbf{G}_{B}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

and hence

\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{B,p}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{B,p}-\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{p}}\right).

The resolvent identity and

\left\lVert\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}},\left\lVert\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{B,n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\rho_{0}^{-1}
yield

\left|\bm{d}_{B,p}^{\top}(\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}-\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{B,n})\bm{d}_{B,p}\right|=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{p}}\right).

(G.22)

In the factor–bulk eigenbasis, write

\mathbf{B}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{B}_{FF,n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{r}&\mathbf{B}_{FB,n}\\
\mathbf{B}_{BF,n}&\mathbf{B}_{BB,n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p-r}\end{pmatrix}.

For a fixed factor coordinate
a\leq r
, set

C_{a,n}=\bm{e}_{a}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{FB,n}\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}\bm{d}_{B,p}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{ai}\bm{Y}_{B,i}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}\bm{d}_{B,p}.

Let
\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}^{(i)}
be the resolvent with column
i
removed. The
Sherman–Morrison identity gives the exact equality

\bm{Y}_{B,i}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}\bm{d}_{B,p}=\frac{\bm{Y}_{B,i}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}^{(i)}\bm{d}_{B,p}}{1+n^{-1}\bm{Y}_{B,i}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}^{(i)}\bm{Y}_{B,i}}.

(G.23)

Conditional on the factor scores and the leave-one-column-out bulk
matrix, the numerator in (
G.23
) is an odd function
of
\bm{G}_{B,i}
and has conditional mean zero. Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{ai}^{2}\{\bm{Y}_{B,i}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}^{(i)}\bm{d}_{B,p}\}^{2}\middle|\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}^{(i)},(G_{bi})_{b\leq r}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq Cp\,\bm{d}_{B,p}^{\top}(\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}^{(i)})^{2}\bm{d}_{B,p}\leq Cp\rho_{0}^{-2}\left\lVert\bm{d}_{B,p}\right\rVert^{2}.

Define the
i
th summand after applying
(
G.23
) by

\zeta_{i,a}=Y_{ai}\frac{\bm{Y}_{B,i}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}^{(i)}\bm{d}_{B,p}}{1+n^{-1}\bm{Y}_{B,i}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}^{(i)}\bm{Y}_{B,i}}.

For
i\neq j
, change only
\bm{G}_{B,i}
to
-\bm{G}_{B,i}
. The
quantity
q_{i,p}
, the matrix
\mathbf{B}_{BB,n}
, every deleted resolvent, and

\zeta_{j,a}
are unchanged, whereas
\zeta_{i,a}
changes sign.
Distributional invariance therefore gives

\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{i,a}\zeta_{j,a})=0
exactly. The denominator is at least
one, and the preceding conditional second-moment bound gives

\mathbb{E}|C_{a,n}|^{2}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\zeta_{i,a}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}p=C\frac{p}{n}\leq C.

(G.24)

Equation (
G.24
) gives
C_{a,n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, so no unexpanded leave-one-column replacement remainder is needed.
Since
r
is fixed,

\left\lVert\mathbf{B}_{FB,n}\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}\bm{d}_{B,p}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

The Schur complement appearing in the inverse of

\mathbf{B}_{n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{p}
satisfies

\left\lVert\{\mathbf{B}_{FF,n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{r}-\mathbf{B}_{FB,n}\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}\mathbf{B}_{BF,n}\}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1})

by Lemma
C.6
. Therefore

\displaystyle\left|\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{d}_{p}-\bm{d}_{B,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}\bm{d}_{B,p}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{B}_{FB,n}\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}\bm{d}_{B,p}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\{\mathbf{B}_{FF,n}+\rho\mathbf{I}_{r}-\mathbf{B}_{FB,n}\mathbf{Q}_{B,n}\mathbf{B}_{BF,n}\}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1}).

(G.25)

It remains to replace the oracle-centered resolvent by the
sample-median-centered resolvent. Lemma
D.4

represents the leading center correction by the columns

\bm{Z}_{n}/\sqrt{n}
and
\bm{W}_{n}/\sqrt{n}
. Conditional on the unsigned
angular observations,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s}\left[\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{Z}_{n}\right\}^{2}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{d}_{p}\leq\frac{1}{n}\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{d}_{p}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s}\left[\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{W}_{n}\right\}^{2}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0},(w_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\bar{w}^{2}}{n}\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{B}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\bm{d}_{p}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}).

Hence

\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{n}\mathbf{H}_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

(G.26)

The two-dimensional middle inverse for the rank-two update is

O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
by Proposition
D.2
and
(
B.18
); hence (
G.26
) makes
its contribution to the quadratic form
O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1})
.
For the factor correction, Lemmas
C.6
,
D.2
, and
D.4
give

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{d}_{p}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}),

(G.27)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{V}_{F,n}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{d}_{p}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(G.28)

The second line cannot generally be improved to
O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})

because
\mathbf{V}_{F,n}
is a bulk vector of order one. Instead, use the
resolvent identity and the factorization

\mathbf{F}_{n}=\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\mathbf{A}_{F,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}+\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\top}+\mathbf{V}_{F,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}.

Every summand contains at least one factor projection, and therefore

\displaystyle\left|\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}(\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}-\mathbf{Q}_{H,n})\bm{d}_{p}\right|

\displaystyle\quad=\left|\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{F}_{n}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{d}_{p}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\lVert\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{F,n}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{d}_{p}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{P}_{F,p}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbf{V}_{F,n}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{d}_{p}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lVert\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{H,n}\mathbf{V}_{F,n}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{F,p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n}\bm{d}_{p}\right\rVert=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2}).

Thus the combined rank-two and factor corrections are

O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}+p^{-1/2})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/2})
.
Finally, Lemma
D.3
and the resolvent identity imply

\left|\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}-\mathbf{Q}_{HF,n})\bm{d}_{p}\right|\leq\rho_{0}^{-2}\left\lVert\bm{d}_{p}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{p}}\right).

(G.29)

Combining (
G.21
),
(
G.22
),
(
G.25
),
(
G.26
),
(
G.27
)–(
G.28
),
and (
G.29
) prove
(
G.14
).

Finally,

\displaystyle\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{B,p}^{\star}(\rho)\bm{d}_{p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho}\sum_{j=r+1}^{p}\frac{(\bm{p}_{j,p}^{\top}\bm{d}_{p})^{2}}{1+\widetilde{\delta}_{p,\rho}\lambda_{j,p}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho}\int\frac{1}{1+\widetilde{\delta}_{p,\rho}t}\,\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}_{p}(t).

(G.30)

The fixed-point variables are contained in a compact subset of

(0,\infty)^{2}
. Every subsequential limit solves
(2.8)–(2.9); uniqueness therefore
gives

\delta_{p,\rho}\to\delta_{\rho},\qquad\widetilde{\delta}_{p,\rho}\to\widetilde{\delta}_{\rho}.

Since the integrand in (
G.30
) is uniformly bounded
and continuous on
[\omega_{-},\omega_{+}]
, the weak convergence

\mathcal{G}_{p}\Rightarrow\mathcal{G}
proves (
G.15
).
∎

Lemma G.4
.

For
a=0,1,2
, define

A_{a,\rho}=\int\frac{d^{1+a/2}}{(1+\delta_{\rho}d)^{2}}\,\,\mathrm{d}F_{D}(d),\qquad C_{a,\rho}=\int\frac{d^{2+a/2}}{(1+\delta_{\rho}d)^{3}}\,\,\mathrm{d}F_{D}(d).

Let

u_{3,\rho}=c\int\frac{t^{3}}{(1+\widetilde{\delta}_{\rho}t)^{3}}\,\,\mathrm{d}H_{B}(t),\qquad v_{3,\rho}=\int\frac{d^{3}}{(1+\delta_{\rho}d)^{3}}\,\,\mathrm{d}F_{D}(d),\qquad S_{\rho}=\rho^{2}-u_{\rho}v_{\rho}.

Then (2.21) holds and

\displaystyle\kappa_{\rho}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\rho}^{\prime}A_{0,\rho},

\displaystyle b_{a,\rho}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\rho}^{\prime}A_{a,\rho},\quad a=1,2,

\displaystyle u_{\rho}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-2\widetilde{\delta}_{\rho}^{\prime}u_{3,\rho},

\displaystyle v_{\rho}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-2\delta_{\rho}^{\prime}v_{3,\rho},

\displaystyle A_{a,\rho}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-2\delta_{\rho}^{\prime}C_{a,\rho},

\displaystyle S_{\rho}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=2\rho-u_{\rho}^{\prime}v_{\rho}-u_{\rho}v_{\rho}^{\prime}.

For
a,b\in\{0,1,2\}
,

\Psi_{ab}^{\prime}(\rho)=m_{a}m_{b}\frac{\{u_{\rho}^{\prime}A_{a,\rho}A_{b,\rho}+u_{\rho}A_{a,\rho}^{\prime}A_{b,\rho}+u_{\rho}A_{a,\rho}A_{b,\rho}^{\prime}\}S_{\rho}-u_{\rho}A_{a,\rho}A_{b,\rho}S_{\rho}^{\prime}}{S_{\rho}^{2}}.

(G.31)

Put
r_{a,\rho}=s_{a}-b_{a,\rho}
,
a=1,2
, and

\bm{h}_{\rho}=\begin{pmatrix}m_{1}^{2}r_{1,\rho}^{2}\\
2\kappa_{\rho}m_{1}r_{1,\rho}\\
\kappa_{\rho}^{2}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\bm{g}_{\rho}=D_{\rho}^{-2}\bm{h}_{\rho}.

Then

\displaystyle D_{\rho}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-2m_{1}^{2}r_{1,\rho}b_{1,\rho}^{\prime}+m_{2}\{\kappa_{\rho}^{\prime}r_{2,\rho}-\kappa_{\rho}b_{2,\rho}^{\prime}\},

\displaystyle\bm{h}_{\rho}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}-2m_{1}^{2}r_{1,\rho}b_{1,\rho}^{\prime}\\
2m_{1}\{\kappa_{\rho}^{\prime}r_{1,\rho}-\kappa_{\rho}b_{1,\rho}^{\prime}\}\\
2\kappa_{\rho}\kappa_{\rho}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{\rho}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=D_{\rho}^{-2}\bm{h}_{\rho}^{\prime}-2D_{\rho}^{-3}D_{\rho}^{\prime}\bm{h}_{\rho}.

Let
\bm{\Gamma}_{\rho}^{\prime}
be obtained from (2.4) by replacing every
\psi_{ab,n}
by
\Psi_{ab}^{\prime}(\rho)
. The derivative of the limiting conditional variance is

\{\sigma_{E}^{2}(\rho)\}^{\prime}=2(\bm{g}_{\rho}^{\prime})^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{\rho}\bm{g}_{\rho}+\bm{g}_{\rho}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{\rho}^{\prime}\bm{g}_{\rho}.

(G.32)

Proof.

Differentiating the two canonical equations under the integral sign gives

\displaystyle\rho\delta_{\rho}^{\prime}+u_{\rho}\widetilde{\delta}_{\rho}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\delta_{\rho},

\displaystyle v_{\rho}\delta_{\rho}^{\prime}+\rho\widetilde{\delta}_{\rho}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\widetilde{\delta}_{\rho},

which is (2.21). By

S_{\rho}\geq s_{0}>0
, the derivative vector is continuous and uniformly bounded on
[\rho_{0},\rho_{1}]
.

For
j\geq 1
and every exponent
\alpha
appearing above,

\frac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}\rho}\int\frac{d^{\alpha}}{(1+\delta_{\rho}d)^{j}}\,\,\mathrm{d}F_{D}(d)=-j\delta_{\rho}^{\prime}\int\frac{d^{\alpha+1}}{(1+\delta_{\rho}d)^{j+1}}\,\,\mathrm{d}F_{D}(d).

The interchange of differentiation and integration follows from

0<d\leq\mu_{-}^{-1}
and
\rho\geq\rho_{0}
. Applying this identity to

\kappa_{\rho}
,
b_{a,\rho}
,
v_{\rho}
, and
A_{a,\rho}
, and applying the analogous identity to the
H_{B}
integral defining
u_{\rho}
, gives the displayed derivative formulas. The quotient rule applied to

\Psi_{ab}(\rho)=m_{a}m_{b}\frac{u_{\rho}A_{a,\rho}A_{b,\rho}}{S_{\rho}}

proves (
G.31
). Direct differentiation of

D_{\rho}
,
\bm{h}_{\rho}
, and
\bm{g}_{\rho}=D_{\rho}^{-2}\bm{h}_{\rho}
gives the remaining vector formulas. Finally,

\frac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}\rho}(\bm{g}_{\rho}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{\rho}\bm{g}_{\rho})=2(\bm{g}_{\rho}^{\prime})^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{\rho}\bm{g}_{\rho}+\bm{g}_{\rho}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{\rho}^{\prime}\bm{g}_{\rho},

which proves (
G.32
).
∎

Proof of Theorem 2.3.

Proposition
G.1
gives the exact identities

\widehat{\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\theta}_{0,p}=\bm{\Delta}_{p}+\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon},\qquad\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{n}=\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}.

It also gives exact equality of the nuisance estimators calculated from
the shifted and noise samples. Consequently,

\displaystyle T_{n}(\rho)-n\widehat{\mu}_{n}={}

\displaystyle\Big\{n\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}-n\widehat{\mu}_{n}\Big\}

\displaystyle+n\bm{\Delta}_{p}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\bm{\Delta}_{p}+2n\bm{\Delta}_{p}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}.

(G.33)

Define the standardized noise contribution by

Z_{\varepsilon,n}(\rho)=\frac{n\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}-n\widehat{\mu}_{n}}{\sqrt{n\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}^{2}}}.

The noise sample satisfies
H_{0}
, so Theorem 2.2 yields

Z_{\varepsilon,n}(\rho)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N(0,1).

(G.34)

Under (2.14),

\bm{\Delta}_{p}=n^{-1/4}\bm{d}_{p}
. Hence

\frac{n\bm{\Delta}_{p}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\bm{\Delta}_{p}}{\sqrt{n}}=\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\bm{d}_{p}.

(G.35)

Lemma
G.3
and
(
G.30
) give

\displaystyle\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\bm{d}_{p}={}

\displaystyle q_{\rho}(\mathcal{G})+O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{p}}\right)+r_{G,p}(\rho),

\displaystyle r_{G,p}(\rho)={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\rho}\int\frac{1}{1+\widetilde{\delta}_{p,\rho}t}\,\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}_{p}(t)-\frac{1}{\rho}\int\frac{1}{1+\widetilde{\delta}_{\rho}t}\,\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}(t)\to 0.

Lemma
G.2
gives

\displaystyle\left|\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}\right|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\left\{\frac{1}{n}\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\bm{d}_{p}\right\}^{1/2}\right]

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}),

where the last equality follows from
(
G.14
) and

\sup_{p}\left\lVert\bm{d}_{p}\right\rVert<\infty
. Therefore

\frac{2n\bm{\Delta}_{p}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{n}}=2n^{1/4}\bm{d}_{p}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/4}).

Let
(\sigma_{E,p}^{\star})^{2}
be defined by (
B.5
). Theorem
F.1
and Proposition
B.1
give

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}^{2}-\sigma_{E}^{2}(\rho)={}

\displaystyle\{\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}^{2}-\sigma_{D,n}^{2}\}+\{\sigma_{D,n}^{2}-(\sigma_{E,p}^{\star})^{2}\}+r_{\sigma,p}(\rho),

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}^{2}-\sigma_{D,n}^{2}={}

\displaystyle O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\sigma_{D,n}^{2}-(\sigma_{E,p}^{\star})^{2}={}

\displaystyle O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/4}),

\displaystyle r_{\sigma,p}(\rho):={}

\displaystyle(\sigma_{E,p}^{\star})^{2}-\sigma_{E}^{2}(\rho)\to 0.

Thus

\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}^{2}=\sigma_{E}^{2}(\rho)+O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/4})+r_{\sigma,p}(\rho).

By (
B.18
) and (2.12),

\sigma_{E}(\rho)>0
for the fixed ridge value under consideration.
The mean-value theorem therefore gives

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}-\sigma_{E}(\rho)={}

\displaystyle O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/4})+O\{|r_{\sigma,p}(\rho)|\},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{E}(\rho)}={}

\displaystyle O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/4})+O\{|r_{\sigma,p}(\rho)|\}.

(G.36)

Divide (
G.33
) by

\sqrt{n\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}^{2}}
and use
(
G.35
)–
(
G.36
). We obtain

\displaystyle Z_{n}(\rho)={}

\displaystyle Z_{\varepsilon,n}(\rho)+\frac{q_{\rho}(\mathcal{G})}{\sigma_{E}(\rho)}

\displaystyle+O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/4})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/4})+O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{p}}\right)+r_{p}(\rho),

(G.37)

where

|r_{p}(\rho)|\leq C\{|r_{G,p}(\rho)|+|r_{\sigma,p}(\rho)|\}\to 0.

Equations (
G.34
) and
(
G.37
) prove
(2.16). Therefore

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}_{H_{1,n}(\mathcal{G})}\{Z_{n}(\rho)>z_{1-\alpha}\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\{N(\Lambda_{\rho}(\mathcal{G}),1)>z_{1-\alpha}\}

\displaystyle=1-\Phi\{z_{1-\alpha}-\Lambda_{\rho}(\mathcal{G})\},

which proves (2.17).

For the consistency statement, define

S_{n,p}=\bm{\Delta}_{p}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\bm{\Delta}_{p}.

Lemma
G.1
gives

S_{n,p}\geq c\left\{\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{B,p}\bm{\Delta}_{p}\right\rVert^{2}+p^{-1}\left\lVert\bm{\Pi}_{F,p}\bm{\Delta}_{p}\right\rVert^{2}\right\}.

Condition (2.19) consequently implies

\sqrt{n}S_{n,p}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\infty.

(G.38)

Lemma
G.2
gives

\displaystyle\frac{2n\bm{\Delta}_{p}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,n}\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{n\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}^{2}}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(S_{n,p}^{1/2}),

\displaystyle\frac{S_{n,p}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}S_{n,p}}

\displaystyle=(nS_{n,p})^{-1/2}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0.

Using (
G.34
), (
B.18
), and
(
G.38
), we obtain

\displaystyle Z_{n}(\rho)={}

\displaystyle Z_{\varepsilon,n}(\rho)+\frac{\sqrt{n}S_{n,p}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(S_{n,p}^{1/2})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{n}S_{n,p}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n}}\left\{1+O_{\mathbb{P}}((nS_{n,p})^{-1/2})\right\}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\infty.

This proves the consistency assertion in Theorem 2.3.
∎

Appendix H
Finite-grid joint limits and Cauchy aggregation

Throughout this appendix,
\mathcal{R}_{K}=\{\rho^{(1)},\ldots,\rho^{(K)}\}
is the fixed grid in Section 3. All maxima over
k
and
\ell
are therefore over a fixed finite set. For compactness, write

\delta_{p,k}=\delta_{p,\rho^{(k)}},\qquad\widetilde{\delta}_{p,k}=\widetilde{\delta}_{p,\rho^{(k)}},\qquad\delta_{k}=\delta_{\rho^{(k)}},\qquad\widetilde{\delta}_{k}=\widetilde{\delta}_{\rho^{(k)}}.

For
1\leq k,\ell\leq K
, define the finite-
p
cross-ridge quantities

\displaystyle u_{p,k\ell}

\displaystyle=c_{B,n}\int\frac{t^{2}}{(1+\widetilde{\delta}_{p,k}t)(1+\widetilde{\delta}_{p,\ell}t)}\,\,\mathrm{d}H_{B,p}(t),

\displaystyle v_{p,k\ell}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{d^{2}}{(1+\delta_{p,k}d)(1+\delta_{p,\ell}d)}\,\,\mathrm{d}F_{D,p}(d),

\displaystyle A_{a,p,k\ell}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{d^{1+a/2}}{(1+\delta_{p,k}d)(1+\delta_{p,\ell}d)}\,\,\mathrm{d}F_{D,p}(d),\qquad a=0,1,2,

and

\Psi_{ab,p}^{k\ell}=m_{a}m_{b}\frac{u_{p,k\ell}A_{a,p,k\ell}A_{b,p,k\ell}}{\rho^{(k)}\rho^{(\ell)}-u_{p,k\ell}v_{p,k\ell}},\qquad a,b\in\{0,1,2\}.

(H.1)

At the limiting level, put

\displaystyle u_{k\ell}

\displaystyle=c\int\frac{t^{2}}{(1+\widetilde{\delta}_{k}t)(1+\widetilde{\delta}_{\ell}t)}\,\,\mathrm{d}H_{B}(t),

\displaystyle v_{k\ell}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{d^{2}}{(1+\delta_{k}d)(1+\delta_{\ell}d)}\,\,\mathrm{d}F_{D}(d),

\displaystyle A_{a,k\ell}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{d^{1+a/2}}{(1+\delta_{k}d)(1+\delta_{\ell}d)}\,\,\mathrm{d}F_{D}(d),

and define
\Psi_{ab}^{k\ell}
by replacing the finite-
p
quantities in (
H.1
) with their limits. Let

\bm{\Gamma}_{k\ell}=\begin{pmatrix}2\Psi_{00}^{k\ell}&2\Psi_{01}^{k\ell}&2\Psi_{11}^{k\ell}\\
2\Psi_{01}^{k\ell}&\Psi_{02}^{k\ell}+\Psi_{11}^{k\ell}&2\Psi_{12}^{k\ell}\\
2\Psi_{11}^{k\ell}&2\Psi_{12}^{k\ell}&2\Psi_{22}^{k\ell}\end{pmatrix}.

(H.2)

Let
\bm{\Gamma}_{p,k\ell}^{\star}
have the form in (
H.2
), with
\Psi_{ab}^{k\ell}
replaced by
\Psi_{ab,p}^{k\ell}
, and write

\bm{g}_{p,k}^{\star}=\bm{g}_{p,\rho^{(k)}}^{\star},\qquad\sigma_{E,p,k}^{\star}=\sigma_{E,p}^{\star}(\rho^{(k)}).

Define the finite-
p
deterministic correlation

r_{p,k\ell}^{\star}=\frac{(\bm{g}_{p,k}^{\star})^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{p,k\ell}^{\star}\bm{g}_{p,\ell}^{\star}}{\sigma_{E,p,k}^{\star}\sigma_{E,p,\ell}^{\star}}.

(H.3)

The notation
\bm{\Gamma}_{\rho^{(k)},\rho^{(\ell)}}
in Section 3 means
\bm{\Gamma}_{k\ell}
. Finally,

r_{k\ell}=\frac{\bm{g}_{\rho^{(k)}}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{k\ell}\bm{g}_{\rho^{(\ell)}}}{\sigma_{E}(\rho^{(k)})\sigma_{E}(\rho^{(\ell)})}.

(H.4)

All factors in (
H.1
) are nonnegative and the stability denominator is positive. Since every component of the two gradient vectors is nonnegative,
r_{p,k\ell}^{\star}\geq 0
and
r_{k\ell}\geq 0
. Positive semidefiniteness and the upper bound one follow from the covariance representation in Lemma
H.2
.

For the oracle sample quantities, let
\mathbf{A}_{n,k}
be the companion matrix
\mathbf{A}_{n}
evaluated at
\rho^{(k)}
, and define

\psi_{ab,n}^{k\ell}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\neq j}(\mathbf{A}_{n,k})_{ij}(\mathbf{A}_{n,\ell})_{ij}w_{i}^{a}w_{j}^{b}.

Let
\bm{\Gamma}_{n}^{k\ell}
have the form in (
H.2
) with
\Psi_{ab}^{k\ell}
replaced by
\psi_{ab,n}^{k\ell}
.

Lemma H.1
.

Under Assumption 1, there is a constant
s_{\times}>0
such that, for all sufficiently large
p
and all
1\leq k,\ell\leq K
,

\rho^{(k)}\rho^{(\ell)}-u_{p,k\ell}v_{p,k\ell}\geq s_{\times}.

(H.5)

Moreover, for every
a,b\in\{0,1,2\}
,

\max_{k,\ell}|\psi_{ab,n}^{k\ell}-\Psi_{ab,p}^{k\ell}|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/4}),

(H.6)

and

\Psi_{ab,p}^{k\ell}\to\Psi_{ab}^{k\ell}.

Consequently,

\max_{k,\ell}\left|\frac{\bm{g}_{n,k}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{n}^{k\ell}\bm{g}_{n,\ell}}{\sigma_{D,n,k}\sigma_{D,n,\ell}}-r_{p,k\ell}^{\star}\right|=O_{\mathbb{P}}\!\left(p^{-1/4}+\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{p}}\right),

(H.7)

where
\bm{g}_{n,k}=\bm{g}_{n}(\rho^{(k)})
and

\sigma_{D,n,k}=\sigma_{D,n}(\rho^{(k)})
. In addition,

\max_{k,\ell}|r_{p,k\ell}^{\star}-r_{k\ell}|\to 0.

Proof.

Cauchy–Schwarz gives

\displaystyle u_{p,k\ell}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq u_{p,kk}u_{p,\ell\ell},

\displaystyle v_{p,k\ell}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq v_{p,kk}v_{p,\ell\ell}.

The proof of Lemma
B.1
supplies one fixed
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that

u_{p,kk}v_{p,kk}\leq\{\rho^{(k)}\}^{2}(1-\varepsilon_{0}),\qquad 1\leq k\leq K.

Hence

\displaystyle u_{p,k\ell}v_{p,k\ell}

\displaystyle\leq\{u_{p,kk}v_{p,kk}u_{p,\ell\ell}v_{p,\ell\ell}\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{(k)}\rho^{(\ell)}(1-\varepsilon_{0}),

which proves (
H.5
) with

s_{\times}=\rho_{0}^{2}\varepsilon_{0}
.

We next evaluate the cross-ridge off-diagonal sum. Condition on the diagonal matrix

\mathbf{D}_{p}^{0}=\operatorname{diag}(D_{1,p}^{0},\ldots,D_{n,p}^{0}).

Retain the separable bulk proxy from (
B.19
) and put

\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}=(\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{B,n}+\rho^{(k)}\mathbf{I}_{p-r})^{-1},\qquad L_{n,k\ell}=\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}(\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,\ell}).

Let
(\delta_{n,k}^{D},\widetilde{\delta}_{n,k}^{D})
be the empirical canonical solution in Lemma
B.2
at
\rho^{(k)}
, and define

\displaystyle u_{n,k\ell}^{D}

\displaystyle=c_{B,n}\int\frac{t^{2}}{(1+\widetilde{\delta}_{n,k}^{D}t)(1+\widetilde{\delta}_{n,\ell}^{D}t)}\,\,\mathrm{d}H_{B,p}(t),

\displaystyle v_{n,k\ell}^{D}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(D_{i,p}^{0})^{2}}{(1+\delta_{n,k}^{D}D_{i,p}^{0})(1+\delta_{n,\ell}^{D}D_{i,p}^{0})}.

We record the feedback calculation rather than invoking a
one-line “two-resolvent expansion.” Put

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{k}

\displaystyle=\{\rho^{(k)}(\mathbf{I}+\widetilde{\delta}_{n,k}^{D}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p})\}^{-1},

\displaystyle\gamma_{i,k}

\displaystyle=(1+\delta_{n,k}^{D}D_{i,p}^{0})^{-1},

\displaystyle\ell_{n,k\ell}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}(\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\mathbf{T}_{k}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\mathbf{T}_{\ell})=\frac{u_{n,k\ell}^{D}}{\rho^{(k)}\rho^{(\ell)}}.

Conditionally on
\mathbf{D}_{p}^{0}
, let

g_{\alpha i}=G_{r+\alpha,i}
and

\lambda_{B,\alpha,p}=\lambda_{r+\alpha,p}
for

1\leq\alpha\leq p-r
, and write

\bm{x}_{i}=(D_{i,p}^{0})^{1/2}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}^{1/2}\bm{g}_{i}
. Then

\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{B,n}=n^{-1}\sum_{i}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}
. The two
identities used in the Gaussian integration-by-parts calculation are

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}}{\partial g_{\alpha i}}

\displaystyle=-\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}\frac{\partial\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{B,n}}{\partial g_{\alpha i}}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k},

(H.8)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{B,n}}{\partial g_{\alpha i}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{D_{i,p}^{0}\lambda_{B,\alpha,p}}}{n}\{\bm{e}_{\alpha}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}+\bm{x}_{i}\bm{e}_{\alpha}^{\top}\}.

(H.9)

Let
\mathbb{E}_{G}(\cdot)=\mathbb{E}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{D}_{p}^{0})
. For each
i
, let

\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}^{(i)}=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}\bm{x}_{j}\bm{x}_{j}^{\top}+\rho^{(k)}\mathbf{I}_{p-r}\right)^{-1}

and define

\displaystyle\widehat{\gamma}_{i,k}

\displaystyle=\left\{1+n^{-1}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}^{(i)}\bm{x}_{i}\right\}^{-1},

\displaystyle L_{n,k\ell}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}(\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}^{(i)}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,\ell}^{(i)}),

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{i,k}

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}^{(i)}\bm{x}_{i}-D_{i,p}^{0}\delta_{n,k}^{D},

\displaystyle\zeta_{i,k\ell}

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}^{(i)}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,\ell}^{(i)}\bm{x}_{i}-D_{i,p}^{0}L_{n,k\ell}^{(i)}.

The Sherman–Morrison formula gives

\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}\bm{x}_{i}=\widehat{\gamma}_{i,k}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}^{(i)}\bm{x}_{i},\qquad|\widehat{\gamma}_{i,k}-\gamma_{i,k}|\leq|\varepsilon_{i,k}|.

We now spell out the feedback calculation. Apply the Gaussian
integration-by-parts identity

\mathbb{E}_{G}(g_{\alpha i}F)=\mathbb{E}_{G}(\partial F/\partial g_{\alpha i})

to the coordinate representation of

n^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,\ell})
, and use
(
H.8
)–(
H.9
)
and the preceding leave-one identities. After summing over
\alpha
, the
zeroth contraction is
\ell_{n,k\ell}^{0}
. The two derivatives falling on
the resolvents give, before replacing the leave-one denominators and
traces,

\frac{\ell_{n,k\ell}^{0}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(D_{i,p}^{0})^{2}\mathbb{E}_{G}\{\widehat{\gamma}_{i,k}\widehat{\gamma}_{i,\ell}L_{n,k\ell}^{(i)}\}.

The remaining contractions contain a centered leave-one quadratic form

\varepsilon_{i,k}
or
\varepsilon_{i,\ell}
, the mixed centered form

\zeta_{i,k\ell}
, or one rank-one deletion error. Since

|\widehat{\gamma}_{i,k}\widehat{\gamma}_{i,\ell}-\gamma_{i,k}\gamma_{i,\ell}|\leq C(|\varepsilon_{i,k}|+|\varepsilon_{i,\ell}|),

collecting these terms yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{G}L_{n,k\ell}={}

\displaystyle\ell_{n,k\ell}^{0}+\frac{\ell_{n,k\ell}^{0}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(D_{i,p}^{0})^{2}\gamma_{i,k}\gamma_{i,\ell}\mathbb{E}_{G}L_{n,k\ell}^{(i)}+\mathcal{R}_{n,k\ell}.

(H.10)

More precisely, Cauchy–Schwarz applied to the displayed centered forms and
the resolvent bounds gives

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}_{n,k\ell}|\leq C\bigg[

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{G}\{|\varepsilon_{i,k}|+|\varepsilon_{i,\ell}|+\varepsilon_{i,k}^{2}+\varepsilon_{i,\ell}^{2}+|\zeta_{i,k\ell}|\}

\displaystyle+\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}_{G}|L_{n,k\ell}^{(i)}-L_{n,k\ell}|+\frac{1}{n}\bigg].

(H.11)

To verify the order in this bound, condition further on all columns except

i
. The Gaussian quadratic-form identity

\operatorname{Var}(\bm{g}^{\top}\mathbf{C}\bm{g})=2\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{C}^{2})
and the uniform bounds on

D_{i,p}^{0}
,
\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}
, and the ridge resolvents give

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{G}\varepsilon_{i,k}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{G}\zeta_{i,k\ell}^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}).

(H.12)

For the first relation, the difference between

n^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}^{(i)})
and

\delta_{n,k}^{D}
is covered by the diagonal bilinear-form estimate in
Theorem 1.1 of
Hachem et al. 2013
; the centered part is
the preceding Gaussian quadratic form. For the second relation, its
conditional mean is zero by the definition of
L_{n,k\ell}^{(i)}
.
Furthermore, the rank-one resolvent identity gives, deterministically,

\max_{i,k,\ell}|L_{n,k\ell}^{(i)}-L_{n,k\ell}|\leq Cn^{-1}.

Cauchy–Schwarz in (
H.11
), together with (
H.12
), therefore
implies

\max_{k,\ell\leq K}|\mathcal{R}_{n,k\ell}|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

(H.13)

For completeness, the random mixed trace itself concentrates at a smaller
order. Direct differentiation gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial L_{n,k\ell}}{\partial g_{\alpha i}}=-\frac{2\sqrt{D_{i,p}^{0}\lambda_{B,\alpha,p}}}{n^{2}}\bm{e}_{\alpha}^{\top}\big(

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,\ell}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}

\displaystyle+\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,\ell}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,\ell}\big)\bm{x}_{i}.

Consequently,

\sum_{\alpha,i}\left|\frac{\partial L_{n,k\ell}}{\partial g_{\alpha i}}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{C}{n^{4}}\sum_{i}\|\bm{x}_{i}\|^{2}.

Conditionally on
\mathbf{D}_{p}^{0}
,

\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}_{G}\|\bm{x}_{i}\|^{2}\leq Cnp=O(n^{2})
. The Gaussian
Poincaré inequality

(
Boucheron et al. 2013
, Theorem 3.20, p. 72)
therefore gives

\operatorname{Var}_{G}(L_{n,k\ell})\leq Cn^{-2}
. Since the grid is fixed,

\max_{k,\ell\leq K}|L_{n,k\ell}-\mathbb{E}_{G}L_{n,k\ell}|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1}).

(H.14)

Using the rank-one deletion bound in
(
H.10
), then
(
H.13
)–(
H.14
),
we obtain

L_{n,k\ell}=\ell_{n,k\ell}^{0}+\ell_{n,k\ell}^{0}v_{n,k\ell}^{D}L_{n,k\ell}+r_{n,k\ell},\qquad\max_{k,\ell\leq K}|r_{n,k\ell}|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

(H.15)

Since
p\asymp n
, the weaker rate needed below is

\displaystyle L_{n,k\ell}={}

\displaystyle\frac{u_{n,k\ell}^{D}}{\rho^{(k)}\rho^{(\ell)}}+\frac{u_{n,k\ell}^{D}v_{n,k\ell}^{D}}{\rho^{(k)}\rho^{(\ell)}}L_{n,k\ell}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/4}).

(H.16)

Equations (
B.6
) and Bernstein’s inequality
(
Boucheron et al. 2013
, Theorem 2.10 and Corollary 2.11, pp. 36–37)
imply

\displaystyle\max_{k,\ell}|u_{n,k\ell}^{D}-u_{p,k\ell}|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\max_{k,\ell}|v_{n,k\ell}^{D}-v_{p,k\ell}|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

The cross-stability bound permits division in (
H.16
), yielding

\max_{k,\ell}\left|L_{n,k\ell}-\frac{u_{p,k\ell}}{\rho^{(k)}\rho^{(\ell)}-u_{p,k\ell}v_{p,k\ell}}\right|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/4}).

(H.17)

Deleting one or two columns changes the normalized trace by
O(p^{-1})
, uniformly over the finite grid, by the resolvent identity and the bound
\|\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\rho_{0}^{-1}
.

Put
\widetilde{w}_{i}=\xi_{i}(D_{i,p}^{0})^{1/2}
and let

\overline{\mathbf{A}}_{n,k}
be the proxy companion matrix at
\rho^{(k)}
. The Efron–Stein inequality
(
Boucheron et al. 2013
, Theorem 3.1, p. 54)
, applied as in (
B.23
) with

(\mathbf{A}_{n})_{ij}^{2}
replaced by

(\mathbf{A}_{n,k})_{ij}(\mathbf{A}_{n,\ell})_{ij}
, gives

\displaystyle\psi_{ab,n}^{k\ell}={}

\displaystyle m_{a}m_{b}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\neq j}(\mathbf{A}_{n,k})_{ij}(\mathbf{A}_{n,\ell})_{ij}\ell_{i,p}^{a}\ell_{j,p}^{b}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

(H.18)

Indeed, changing one
\xi_{i}
changes the displayed function by at most

\displaystyle\frac{C}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}|(\mathbf{A}_{n,k})_{ij}(\mathbf{A}_{n,\ell})_{ij}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\left\{\sum_{j}(\mathbf{A}_{n,k})_{ij}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\left\{\sum_{j}(\mathbf{A}_{n,\ell})_{ij}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}.

Replacing
\ell_{i,p}
by
(D_{i,p}^{0})^{1/2}
costs

O_{\mathbb{P}}\{(\log p/p)^{1/2}\}
by (
A.3
). For the companion matrices,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j}|(\mathbf{A}_{n,k})_{ij}(\mathbf{A}_{n,\ell})_{ij}-(\overline{\mathbf{A}}_{n,k})_{ij}(\overline{\mathbf{A}}_{n,\ell})_{ij}|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{n}\|\mathbf{A}_{n,k}-\overline{\mathbf{A}}_{n,k}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{A}_{n,\ell}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\frac{1}{n}\|\overline{\mathbf{A}}_{n,k}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{A}_{n,\ell}-\overline{\mathbf{A}}_{n,\ell}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
