\displaystyle\quad=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{p}}+n^{-1/2}\right),

where (
B.20
), (
B.21
), and the contraction bounds were used.

It remains to evaluate

T_{ab,n}^{k\ell}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\neq j}(\overline{\mathbf{A}}_{n,k})_{ij}(\overline{\mathbf{A}}_{n,\ell})_{ij}(D_{i,p}^{0})^{a/2}(D_{j,p}^{0})^{b/2}.

For
i\neq j
, let
\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}^{(ij)}
be the ridge-
\rho^{(k)}
resolvent after deleting columns
i
and
j
. The two-column Schur complement gives

(\overline{\mathbf{A}}_{n,k})_{ij}=\frac{n^{-1}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}^{(ij)}\bm{x}_{j}}{\{1+\delta_{n,k}^{D}D_{i,p}^{0}\}\{1+\delta_{n,k}^{D}D_{j,p}^{0}\}}+r_{ij,k},

where the averaged squared contribution of
r_{ij,k}
to

T_{ab,n}^{k\ell}
is
O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
. Put

q_{ij,k}=\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}^{(ij)}\bm{x}_{j},\qquad\gamma_{i,k}=(1+\delta_{n,k}^{D}D_{i,p}^{0})^{-1}.

After the denominator replacement, define

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{ab,n}^{k\ell}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{i\neq j}(D_{i,p}^{0})^{a/2}(D_{j,p}^{0})^{b/2}\gamma_{i,k}\gamma_{i,\ell}\gamma_{j,k}\gamma_{j,\ell}q_{ij,k}q_{ij,\ell},

\displaystyle M_{ab,n}^{k\ell}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{i\neq j}(D_{i,p}^{0})^{1+a/2}(D_{j,p}^{0})^{1+b/2}\gamma_{i,k}\gamma_{i,\ell}\gamma_{j,k}\gamma_{j,\ell}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\operatorname{tr}(\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}^{(ij)}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,\ell}^{(ij)}).

The averaged denominator error stated above gives

T_{ab,n}^{k\ell}-\widetilde{T}_{ab,n}^{k\ell}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
. Conditional on the deleted resolvents and

\mathbf{D}_{p}^{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{i,j}(q_{ij,k}q_{ij,\ell})=D_{i,p}^{0}D_{j,p}^{0}\operatorname{tr}(\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}^{(ij)}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,\ell}^{(ij)}),

so the conditional expectation of

\widetilde{T}_{ab,n}^{k\ell}-M_{ab,n}^{k\ell}
is zero. Differentiating

q_{ij,k}q_{ij,\ell}
and the mixed trace with respect to the Gaussian
coordinates gives the same three derivative classes as in the proof of
Proposition
B.1
, with one ridge-
\rho^{(k)}
and one
ridge-
\rho^{(\ell)}
resolvent. The bounds

\|\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}^{(ij)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\rho_{0}^{-1}
and

\|\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\omega_{+}
, followed by the same
Cauchy–Schwarz and Gaussian moment calculation, give

\mathbb{E}_{G}\left[\sum_{\alpha=1}^{p-r}\sum_{h=1}^{n}\left|\frac{\partial(\widetilde{T}_{ab,n}^{k\ell}-M_{ab,n}^{k\ell})}{\partial g_{\alpha h}}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{C}{n}

uniformly over the fixed grid. Its conditional mean is zero, so the
Gaussian Poincaré inequality implies

\widetilde{T}_{ab,n}^{k\ell}-M_{ab,n}^{k\ell}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

Finally, deleting two columns changes the unnormalized mixed trace by at
most a constant. Hence, uniformly over
i\neq j
and the fixed grid,

\operatorname{tr}(\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,k}^{(ij)}\bm{\Lambda}_{B,p}\overline{\mathbf{R}}_{n,\ell}^{(ij)})=nL_{n,k\ell}+O(1).

Summing the
O(1)
error contributes
O(n^{-1})
, and restoring the
omitted diagonal contributes the same order. Thus

\displaystyle T_{ab,n}^{k\ell}={}

\displaystyle L_{n,k\ell}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\frac{(D_{i,p}^{0})^{1+a/2}}{(1+\delta_{n,k}^{D}D_{i,p}^{0})(1+\delta_{n,\ell}^{D}D_{i,p}^{0})}\right\}

\displaystyle\times\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j}\frac{(D_{j,p}^{0})^{1+b/2}}{(1+\delta_{n,k}^{D}D_{j,p}^{0})(1+\delta_{n,\ell}^{D}D_{j,p}^{0})}\right\}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

Using (
H.17
), the empirical fixed-point rate, and Bernstein’s inequality
(
Boucheron et al. 2013
, Theorem 2.10 and Corollary 2.11, pp. 36–37)
in the two averages gives

T_{ab,n}^{k\ell}=\frac{u_{p,k\ell}A_{a,p,k\ell}A_{b,p,k\ell}}{\rho^{(k)}\rho^{(\ell)}-u_{p,k\ell}v_{p,k\ell}}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/4}).

Together with (
H.18
), this proves (
H.6
).

Equations (
B.13
)–(
B.17
) give

\displaystyle\max_{k}\|\bm{g}_{n,k}-\bm{g}_{p,k}^{\star}\|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{p}}\right),

\displaystyle\max_{k}|\sigma_{D,n,k}-\sigma_{E,p,k}^{\star}|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/4}).

The finite-
p
denominators are uniformly bounded away from zero. Substitution into (
H.3
) proves (
H.7
).

The compact canonical bounds, weak convergence of
H_{B,p}
and

F_{D,p}
, and uniqueness of the limiting canonical equations give, for every fixed
k
and
\ell
,

\Psi_{ab,p}^{k\ell}\to\Psi_{ab}^{k\ell},\qquad\bm{g}_{p,k}^{\star}\to\bm{g}_{\rho^{(k)}},\qquad\sigma_{E,p,k}^{\star}\to\sigma_{E}(\rho^{(k)}).

Because
K
is fixed, the convergence is uniform over the grid. Equation (
H.3
) therefore gives

\max_{k,\ell}|r_{p,k\ell}^{\star}-r_{k\ell}|\to 0
.
∎

For each
k
, let

\bm{q}_{n,k}=(x_{n,k},y_{n,k},z_{n,k})^{\top},\qquad\bm{q}_{0,n,k}=(\kappa_{n,k},b_{1,n,k},b_{2,n,k})^{\top},

where the quantities in
E
are evaluated at

\rho^{(k)}
. The exact sign identities become

\displaystyle x_{n,k}-\kappa_{n,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij,k}s_{i}s_{j},

\displaystyle y_{n,k}-b_{1,n,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij,k}(w_{i}+w_{j})s_{i}s_{j},

\displaystyle z_{n,k}-b_{2,n,k}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i<j}a_{ij,k}w_{i}w_{j}s_{i}s_{j},

where
a_{ij,k}
is the
(i,j)
entry of the unsigned companion matrix at
\rho^{(k)}
.

Lemma H.2
.

Under Assumption 1 and
H_{0}
, conditionally on

\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
,

\sqrt{n}\{\bm{q}_{n,1}-\bm{q}_{0,n,1},\ldots,\bm{q}_{n,K}-\bm{q}_{0,n,K}\}

has a centered Gaussian approximation in probability whose
(k,\ell)
covariance block is
\bm{\Gamma}_{n}^{k\ell}
. More precisely, for every bounded
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
-measurable triangular array

\bm{c}_{n,k}=(c_{1,n,k},c_{2,n,k},c_{3,n,k})^{\top}
,

L_{n}=\sqrt{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{c}_{n,k}^{\top}(\bm{q}_{n,k}-\bm{q}_{0,n,k})

satisfies

\frac{L_{n}}{V_{n}^{1/2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N(0,1)\quad\text{conditionally in probability}

whenever

V_{n}=\sum_{k,\ell=1}^{K}\bm{c}_{n,k}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{n}^{k\ell}\bm{c}_{n,\ell}

is bounded away from zero. If
V_{n}\to 0
, then
L_{n}\to 0
in conditional probability.

Proof.

The exact covariance calculation in Lemma
E.2
applies to two different ridge values because only equal unordered index pairs survive. It gives

n\operatorname{Cov}_{s}(\bm{q}_{n,k},\bm{q}_{n,\ell}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})=\bm{\Gamma}_{n}^{k\ell}.

For the stated projection, put

h_{ij,n,k}=2c_{1,n,k}+c_{2,n,k}(w_{i}+w_{j})+2c_{3,n,k}w_{i}w_{j}.

Then

L_{n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i<j}\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{ij,k}h_{ij,n,k}\right\}s_{i}s_{j}.

(H.19)

Let
\mathbf{C}_{n}
be symmetric with zero diagonal and

(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{ij,k}h_{ij,n,k},\qquad i\neq j.

Since
0<w_{i}\leq\bar{w}
,
K
is fixed, and the coefficient vectors are bounded,

|h_{ij,n,k}|\leq C
. As in (
E.6
), the matrix before removal of its diagonal is a fixed finite sum of

\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n,k},\qquad\mathbf{D}_{w}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n,k}+\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n,k}\mathbf{D}_{w},\qquad\mathbf{D}_{w}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n,k}\mathbf{D}_{w}.

Each companion matrix is a contraction. Therefore

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{C}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{-1/2},

(H.20)

\displaystyle\max_{i}\sum_{j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{CK}{n}\max_{i,k}\sum_{j}a_{ij,k}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{n},

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\max_{i}\sum_{j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{2}\right\}\|\mathbf{C}_{n}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(H.21)

The conditional variance of (
H.19
) is

2\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{C}_{n}^{2})=\sum_{k,\ell=1}^{K}\bm{c}_{n,k}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{n}^{k\ell}\bm{c}_{n,\ell}=V_{n}.

The graph expansion used in the proof of Theorem
E.1
, together with
(
H.20
)–(
H.21
), gives

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{s}(L_{n}^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})-3V_{n}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{C}_{n}^{4})+C\sum_{i}\left\{\sum_{j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{2}\right\}^{2}+C\sum_{i,j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{-1}\{1+V_{n}\}.

On
\{V_{n}\geq\eta\}
,

\frac{\max_{i}\sum_{j}(\mathbf{C}_{n})_{ij}^{2}}{V_{n}}\leq\frac{C}{n\eta},\qquad\left|\frac{\mathbb{E}_{s}(L_{n}^{4}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})}{V_{n}^{2}}-3\right|\leq\frac{C}{n\eta^{2}}.

Theorem 2.1 of
de Jong 1987
gives the conditional normal approximation uniformly on this event. On
\{V_{n}<\eta\}
,

\mathbb{E}_{s}(|L_{n}|\mid\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0})\leq\sqrt{\eta}.

Letting
n\to\infty
and then
\eta\downarrow 0
proves the assertion.
∎

Proof of Theorem 3.1.

For each
k
, define

\varphi_{n,k}(x,y,z)=\frac{x}{e_{n}^{2}+t_{n}x-2e_{n}y+y^{2}-xz}.

At
\bm{q}_{0,n,k}
,

\nabla\varphi_{n,k}(\bm{q}_{0,n,k})=\bm{g}_{n,k},\qquad\varphi_{n,k}(\bm{q}_{0,n,k})=\mu_{n,k}.

The compact bounds in Proposition
B.1
hold
simultaneously over the fixed grid. The exact covariance calculation
and
K<\infty
give

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\|\bm{q}_{n,k}-\bm{q}_{0,n,k}\|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}|L_{k}\{\bm{q}_{0,n,k}+t(\bm{q}_{n,k}-\bm{q}_{0,n,k})\}-D_{n,k}|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}),

where
L_{k}
denotes
L(\cdot,e_{n},t_{n})
at ridge

\rho^{(k)}
. Hence, with probability tending to one, every
intermediate denominator is at least one half of the common positive
lower bound. Taylor’s theorem then gives

\displaystyle\max_{k}\left|\sqrt{n}\{\varphi_{n,k}(\bm{q}_{n,k})-\mu_{n,k}-\bm{g}_{n,k}^{\top}(\bm{q}_{n,k}-\bm{q}_{0,n,k})\}\right|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

Lemma
H.2
, applied with the
K
gradients, shows that the vector

\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}\{\varphi_{n,k}(\bm{q}_{n,k})-\mu_{n,k}\}}{\sigma_{D,n,k}}\right\}_{k=1}^{K}

has a conditional Gaussian approximation with correlation matrix

\mathbf{C}_{K,n}^{0}=\left\{\frac{\bm{g}_{n,k}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{n}^{k\ell}\bm{g}_{n,\ell}}{\sigma_{D,n,k}\sigma_{D,n,\ell}}\right\}_{k,\ell=1}^{K}.

Lemma
H.1
gives

\|\mathbf{C}_{K,n}^{0}-\mathbf{C}_{K}\|_{\max}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Proposition
D.2
and the proof of
Theorem 2.1 give, at every grid point,

T_{n}(\rho^{(k)})=n\varphi_{n,k}(\bm{q}_{n,k})+R_{T,n,k},\qquad R_{T,n,k}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1+\log p).

Because
K
is fixed,

\max_{k}\frac{|R_{T,n,k}|}{\sqrt{n}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}\{(1+\log p)/\sqrt{n}\}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Theorem
F.1
also holds simultaneously over the grid and yields

\displaystyle\max_{k}\sqrt{n}|\widehat{\mu}_{n,k}-\mu_{n,k}|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle\max_{k}\left|\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{D,n,k}^{2}}{\sigma_{D,n,k}^{2}}-1\right|

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

Conditional Slutsky’s theorem, followed by integration over

\mathcal{F}_{n}^{0}
, proves

(Z_{n,1},\ldots,Z_{n,K})^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N_{K}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{C}_{K}).

∎

Lemma H.3
.

Let
\bm{V}\sim N_{K}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{C}_{K})
and let

Y_{k}=\tan[\pi\{\Phi(V_{k})-1/2\}],\qquad S_{K}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\varpi_{k}Y_{k}.

For every deterministic
\bm{a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{K})^{\top}
, the random variable

S_{K}(\bm{a})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\varpi_{k}\tan[\pi\{\Phi(V_{k}+a_{k})-1/2\}]

has a continuous distribution with a strictly increasing distribution function. In addition,

\mathbb{P}(S_{K}>t)=\frac{1+o(1)}{\pi t},\qquad t\to\infty.

(H.22)

Proof.

By (
H.1
)–(
H.4
), the matrix

\mathbf{C}_{K}
is symmetric, positive semidefinite, entrywise nonnegative,
and has unit diagonal. The Perron–Frobenius theorem therefore gives
an eigenvalue
\lambda_{\star}>0
and a unit eigenvector

\bm{h}=(h_{1},\ldots,h_{K})^{\top}\geq\mathbf{0}
. A Gaussian spectral
decomposition yields

\bm{V}=\sqrt{\lambda_{\star}}\,\bm{h}\,Z+\bm{V}^{\perp},\qquad Z\sim N(0,1),\qquad Z\text{ is independent of }\bm{V}^{\perp}.

Write
\psi(x)=\tan[\pi\{\Phi(x)-1/2\}]
. Conditionally on

\bm{V}^{\perp}=\bm{v}
, for any deterministic
\bm{a}
,

S_{K}(\bm{a})=g_{\bm{v},\bm{a}}(Z),\qquad g_{\bm{v},\bm{a}}(z)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\varpi_{k}\psi(v_{k}+a_{k}+\sqrt{\lambda_{\star}}h_{k}z).

Since

\psi^{\prime}(x)=\pi\phi(x)\sec^{2}[\pi\{\Phi(x)-1/2\}]>0,

and at least one
h_{k}
is positive,

g_{\bm{v},\bm{a}}^{\prime}(z)=\sqrt{\lambda_{\star}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\varpi_{k}h_{k}\psi^{\prime}(v_{k}+a_{k}+\sqrt{\lambda_{\star}}h_{k}z)>0.

Moreover,
g_{\bm{v},\bm{a}}(z)\to\pm\infty
as

z\to\pm\infty
. Thus its conditional distribution is continuous
and assigns positive probability to every nonempty interval.
Integration over
\bm{V}^{\perp}
proves the first assertion.

It remains to establish the right-tail expansion at
\bm{a}=\mathbf{0}
.
Every
Y_{k}
is standard Cauchy because
\Phi(V_{k})
is uniform on

(0,1)
. If
r_{k\ell}=1
, then
V_{k}=V_{\ell}
almost surely. Merge all indices connected by this relation and replace their weights by the sum of the weights in the class. This leaves
S_{K}
unchanged. After merging, write the number of classes as
J
, their positive weights as
\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{J}
, and the corresponding Gaussian variables as
W_{1},\ldots,W_{J}
. Then

\sum_{j=1}^{J}\omega_{j}=1,\qquad 0\leq\operatorname{Corr}(W_{j},W_{\ell})<1\quad(j\neq\ell).

If
J=1
, then
\omega_{1}=1
and
S_{K}=Y_{1}
is standard Cauchy. Hence

\mathbb{P}(S_{K}>t)=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\pi}\arctan(t)=\frac{1+o(1)}{\pi t},

which proves (
H.22
) in this case. We may therefore
assume
J\geq 2
in the remainder of the proof.
Put

X_{j}=\omega_{j}\tan[\pi\{\Phi(W_{j})-1/2\}].

The marginal Cauchy tail gives, for every fixed
a>0
,

\mathbb{P}(X_{j}>at)=\frac{\omega_{j}}{\pi at}+O(t^{-3}),\qquad\mathbb{P}(X_{j}<-at)=\frac{\omega_{j}}{\pi at}+O(t^{-3}).

We first verify asymptotic separation of distinct extremes. For each

j
and fixed
a>0
, let

u_{j,t}(a)=\pi^{-1}\operatorname{arccot}(at/\omega_{j})
and

z_{j,t}(a)=\Phi^{-1}\{1-u_{j,t}(a)\}
. Mills’ ratio gives

z_{j,t}(a)^{2}=2\log t+O(\log\log t).

For
j\neq\ell
, put
r=\operatorname{Corr}(W_{j},W_{\ell})<1
. The Gaussian exponential bound for an upper orthant yields, for fixed
a,b>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X_{j}>at,X_{\ell}>bt)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left[-\frac{z_{j,t}(a)^{2}-2rz_{j,t}(a)z_{\ell,t}(b)+z_{\ell,t}(b)^{2}}{2(1-r^{2})}\right]

\displaystyle=t^{-2/(1+r)+o(1)}=o(t^{-1}).

Replacing
W_{\ell}
by
-W_{\ell}
gives

\mathbb{P}(X_{j}>at,X_{\ell}<-bt)=t^{-2/(1-r)+o(1)}=o(t^{-1}).

(H.23)

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
. If

X_{j}>(1+\varepsilon)t,\qquad X_{\ell}\geq-\frac{\varepsilon t}{J-1}\quad\text{for every }\ell\neq j,

then
\sum_{j=1}^{J}X_{j}>t
. Inclusion–exclusion,
(
H.23
), and the positive joint-tail bound give

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{J}X_{j}>t\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}\{X_{j}>(1+\varepsilon)t\}+o(t^{-1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1+o(1)}{\pi(1+\varepsilon)t}.

For the upper bound, suppose

\sum_{j}X_{j}>t
and
\max_{j}X_{j}\leq(1-\varepsilon)t
. The largest summand is larger than
t/J
. If its index is
j
, the remaining summands have sum exceeding
\varepsilon t
, so at least one

\ell\neq j
satisfies
X_{\ell}>\varepsilon t/(J-1)
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{J}X_{j}>t\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\{\max_{j}X_{j}>(1-\varepsilon)t\}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j\neq\ell}\mathbb{P}\left\{X_{j}>t/J,X_{\ell}>\varepsilon t/(J-1)\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{P}\{X_{j}>(1-\varepsilon)t\}+o(t^{-1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1+o(1)}{\pi(1-\varepsilon)t}.

Letting
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
proves
(
H.22
).
∎

Proof of Theorem 3.2.

Define the continuous map

\mathcal{T}(\bm{z})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\varpi_{k}\tan[\pi\{\Phi(z_{k})-1/2\}],\qquad\bm{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}.

Theorem 3.1 and the continuous mapping theorem give

T_{\mathrm{CC},n}=\mathcal{T}(Z_{n,1},\ldots,Z_{n,K})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}\mathcal{T}(\bm{V})=T_{\mathrm{CC},\infty}^{0}.

(H.24)

Therefore, at every continuity point
c
,

\mathbb{P}(T_{\mathrm{CC},n}>c)\to\mathbb{P}(T_{\mathrm{CC},\infty}^{0}>c).

Taking
c=c_{\alpha,K}
in the preceding display gives

\mathbb{P}(T_{\mathrm{CC},n}>c_{\alpha,K})\to\alpha.

For the analytic
p
-value,

p_{\mathrm{CC},n}\leq\alpha\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad T_{\mathrm{CC},n}\geq\cot(\pi\alpha).

Equation (
H.24
) gives the asserted fixed-
\alpha
limit. Lemma
H.3
and

\cot(\pi\alpha)=\frac{1}{\pi\alpha}\{1+O(\alpha^{2})\},\qquad\alpha\downarrow 0,

yield

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\downarrow 0}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{P}(p_{\mathrm{CC},n}\leq\alpha)}{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\alpha\downarrow 0}\frac{\mathbb{P}\{T_{\mathrm{CC},\infty}^{0}>\cot(\pi\alpha)\}}{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\alpha\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{\pi\alpha\cot(\pi\alpha)}=1.

∎

Proof of Theorem 3.3.

Under the local alternative (2.14), translation invariance in Proposition
G.1
implies that all nuisance estimators have exactly their null distributions. Equation (
G.37
), evaluated at the fixed grid points, gives

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\left|Z_{n,k}-Z_{\varepsilon,n}(\rho^{(k)})-\Lambda_{\rho^{(k)}}(\mathcal{G})\right|

\displaystyle\leq O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/4})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(p^{-1/4})

\displaystyle\quad+O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{p}}\right)+\max_{k}|r_{p}(\rho^{(k)})|

\displaystyle=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

where the last equality uses
K<\infty
. The noise vector

\{Z_{\varepsilon,n}(\rho^{(k)})\}_{k=1}^{K}
has the joint null limit in Theorem 3.1. Slutsky’s theorem therefore gives

(Z_{n,1},\ldots,Z_{n,K})^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N_{K}\{\bm{\Lambda}_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{G}),\mathbf{C}_{K}\}.

The continuous mapping theorem applied to
\mathcal{T}
gives convergence of the aggregate. The shifted continuity assertion in Lemma
H.3
shows that both rejection thresholds are continuity points of the limiting distribution, which proves the two stated power formulas.

Under (2.18) and (2.19), the consistency part of Theorem 2.3 gives, for every fixed
k
and every
M<\infty
,

\mathbb{P}\{Z_{n}(\rho^{(k)})\leq M\}\to 0.

Since
K
is fixed,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\min_{1\leq k\leq K}Z_{n}(\rho^{(k)})\leq M\right\}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{P}\{Z_{n}(\rho^{(k)})\leq M\}\to 0.

Thus
p_{n,k}\to 0
simultaneously and

\min_{k}\tan[\pi\{1/2-p_{n,k}\}]\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\infty.

Because all weights are positive,

T_{\mathrm{CC},n}\geq\min_{k}\tan[\pi\{1/2-p_{n,k}\}]\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\infty.

Consequently,

p_{\mathrm{CC},n}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\pi}\arctan(T_{\mathrm{CC},n})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0.

The rejection probability tends to one.
∎
